# Iron Boot Test Log — Base Era

Append-only running log of AGNOS iron-boot work for the **base** phase of the
maturity arc ([[project_agnos_maturity_arc]]: demo → base → server → desktop →
swallow). The **MVP gate** (boot-to-shell with a typeable keyboard) closed at
Attempt 68 (1.30.9); the **MVP 2.0** networking + write era closed with the
1.32.x/1.33.x iron burns. This log covers the **base-hardening** software work
on archaemenid — filesystem depth + the slimming/perf runway:

- **big-write own-cycles** — ext4 **extent allocation** (1.37.x), **jbd2
  journaling** (1.38.x), the **VFS generic-write** lift (1.39.x);
- **kernel-slimming + perf** — font → `kashi`, shell → `agnoshi`, then the
  perf + agnos-2.0 runway (1.40.x – 1.45.x).

**Planned log separations** (user, 2026-05-27): the iron logs split along the
maturity arc — **base → server → platforms**. This is the **base** log; a
**server**-era log opens when server-grade work begins, and the **platform
decades** (1.5x Intel / 1.6x Pi-ARM / 1.7x radios / 1.8x RISC-V — a distinct
per-hardware-target testing surface) get their own **platforms** log(s) rather
than landing here.

**Format** (per [[feedback_retire_attempt_counter_post_mvp]]): new iron-burn
entries headline by **version + subsystem**, not an Attempt-N counter (the
counter is retired post-MVP; past `Attempt N` anchors live in the archives).
Never rewrite past entries; add a clarifying note to a later entry pointing
back. Status is one of `FAIL` / `PASS` / `PARTIAL` / `PENDING`.

## Hypothesis & Expectations Tracker — by version cycle

> **Purpose**: reduce session-restart context-reload cost. State.md's "current scope" pointer + this tracker = quick "where are we, what's the open hypothesis, what does the next burn need to show to confirm/falsify it." The chronological per-version narrative stays below in `## Burns`; this section is the **predictive layer**, not a duplicate changelog.
>
> Per [[feedback_iron_burns_block_other_work]] + [[feedback_known_knowledge_first]] + [[feedback_redesign_dont_reinvent]]: each open cycle's hypothesis lists the **falsification criteria** that govern what we do next — never propose a burn without an expected-vs-fallback rubric written here first.
>
> **State.md cycle headers link to these anchors via `iron-nuc-zen-log.md#tracker-1373-cycle` style.** Archived (MVP2.0-era) trackers live in [`iron-nuc-zen-log-mvp2.md`](iron-nuc-zen-log-mvp2.md).

### Tracker: 1.51.x cycle — sovereign-package-manager kernel surface (ark v2 / agnova prerequisite gaps). OPENED 2026-06-29. **LOW iron exposure — no flagged burn yet.** {#tracker-151x-cycle}

This arc is syscalls + ext2 + exec-machinery (QEMU-complete), not the iron-only-risk class the RAM/SMP arcs were. **(a) `symlink`#63 agnos-arm landed 1.51.0** — `ext2_symlink` (already e2fsck-clean via the ext2-write selftest `Wsym fast`/`Wsym slow`) exposed as syscall #63. It is **⚠ two-sided**: ring 3 can't call it until cyrius adds the `sys_symlink`#63 peer (hands-off, flagged to the user), so there's nothing for ring 3 to exercise on iron yet. **The eventual iron touch for this arc is the on-agnos `.ark`-install round-trip** (ark M3) — a symlink-bearing package installing + resolving on the agnos-fs — which is gated on the ark/agnova chain, not a standalone kernel burn. Remaining items (b boot-slot / c nested-exec / d argv-cap / e build-confinement) are filed + gated on the ark self-host milestones; (b) carries an iron implication (atomic A/B update) when its design call is made. **No burn is requested for 1.51.0** — it rides whenever the next agnos burn happens (or the ark-install milestone), per [[feedback_iron_burns_block_other_work]].

### Tracker: 1.50.x cycle — RAM full-usage continuation: full-RAM stress de-risk + boot-CR3 → own-complete-PML4 switch (✅ **ARC CLOSED + IRON-VALIDATED 2026-06-29** on real 64 GB Zen — bites 1+2, mmap-arena lift, process-isolation hardening, and the agnsh-`&` fix (1.50.9/10) all burned green through 1.50.10). {#tracker-150x-cycle}

**▶ 1.50.9 + 1.50.10 — agnsh `&` background jobs RESTORED. ✅ IRON-VALIDATED 2026-06-29 on real 64 GB Zen (FB: `[ASSIST] >` live → `iam &` → `[1] 6` → `[1] Done`, prompt stayed live + `iam | anuenue` rainbow-piped, `Memory: 61 GiB`). The real bug was NOT `spawn_path`#43 — CORRECTION to the 1.50.8 entry below, which called the agnsh-bg failure "the pre-existing `spawn_path#43` breakage." That framing was WRONG.** #43 is correct (`exec-smoke`'s `ring3: spawnpath OK` PASSES; the 30 s-default "fail" was a harness timeout — the `RING3_SELFTEST` proc B counts to 2²⁴ under TCG, so `exec-smoke`'s default `QEMU_TIMEOUT` is now 90). agnsh never reached a prompt to *issue* `&`.

- **Root cause (QEMU, 2026-06-29 — monitor register/stack reads + `-d int`, [[feedback_disassemble_before_theorizing_hangs]]):** agnsh died with a `#PF` (`CR2=0`, `e=0x7`) right after its banner — its first heap `alloc` → `sys_mmap`#27 returned **0**. The migrated RAM-backed PMM bitmap lives at phys **`0x400000`** (the ≤256 MB pool) and was reached by its **identity VA**, but `0x400000` is the FIXED ring-3 code VA (`PD[2]`) that EVERY per-proc CR3 overrides. A syscall runs under the **caller's** CR3, so `pmm_count_2mb_free()` read agnsh's ELF *code* (≈40 % of bits set → "0 free 2 MB regions"). The null-deref then **HUNG the box** at the 1.46.x SMP reap fence (`proc_reap_off_cpu_fence` spun forever reaping the preemptive foreground agnsh, whose `on_cpu` was still set).
- **Regression window = 1.50.3–1.50.6** (the mmap/NX rework — `proc_map_page_nx` / PF_X-aware ELF / per-proc cursor). **NOT 1.44.x** (the original report's guess) and **NOT 1.50.7/1.50.8** (verified by stashing those). KEY DATUM: **the 1.50.1/1.50.2 iron burns booted agnsh to `[ASSIST] >` and ran `iam` on this same 64 GB box** — so agnsh's mmap WORKED on iron at 1.50.2; the bug came after. **⟹ this is the first iron burn since 1.50.2 to re-exercise agnsh interactively, and the first ever to test `&`.** (Exact-cut bisect within 1.50.3–6 not run — offered; the fix is window-independent.)
- **Fix (1.50.9, QEMU-verified — agnsh boots + `&` works with it, #PFs without):** rebase `pmm_bitmap_ptr` onto the **direct-map** (`DIRECTMAP_BASE + phys`) after the boot-CR3 switch — the `PDPT[8+]` window every per-proc CR3 mirrors and never overrides, so the bitmap reads correctly under EVERY CR3; **+** the reap fence clears `on_cpu` for a THIS-CPU-owned (kernel_resume-terminated) proc instead of spinning. **Hardening (1.50.10):** always-on `pmm: bitmap-dm OK free2mb=N` guard, KEEP_GNOBOOT warning, the fence self-owned check folded into `PPID_SELFTEST`, sibling audit (the bitmap was the UNIQUE `PD[2]` victim — only override in the ≤256 MB identity range).

  - **Hypothesis (1.50.10 BARE production kernel on 64 GB Zen):** agnsh's heap mmap now works under its own per-proc CR3 → agnsh reaches `[ASSIST] >` (no `#PF`-freeze after the banner), the always-on guard prints `pmm: bitmap-dm OK free2mb=N` with N large, and `&` jobs launch via #43 + reap. The bitmap guard is in the **production path** (no selftest flag needed for the dispositive signal).
  - **✅ PASS (read the FB — [[feedback_fb_logs_over_cmos]] [[feedback_no_serial_on_iron]]):**
    1. RAM init prints **`pmm: bitmap-dm OK free2mb=N`** with N in the tens of thousands (~32700 for ~62 GB usable) — **NOT** a `WARN bitmap on identity VA` line, **NOT** N=0/garbage.
    2. the `agnoshi 1.8.x` banner is FOLLOWED by **`[ASSIST] >`** — agnsh did NOT freeze/canary after the banner (the per-proc-CR3 mmap proof — the actual bug scenario, which the boot-context guard line in (1) does NOT cover).
    3. type **`iam &`** (any `/bin` tool + `&`) → **`[1] <pid>`** prints + the prompt returns + later **`[1] Done`** (the `&` job launched non-blocking via #43 and reaped).
    4. type **`exit`** → `bye` → kybernet continues (NOT a freeze) — the reap-fence self-owned fix held on a foreground reap.
  - **❌ FALSIFIES → NEXT:** (a) banner → FB FREEZE/canary, no `[ASSIST] >` ⟹ agnsh still `#PF`'d (mmap=0 under the per-proc CR3) — the direct-map rebase didn't take on silicon; the split between (1) printing OK (boot/0x1000 context) and (2) freezing (syscall/per-proc-CR3 context) localizes it to the per-proc mirror of the direct-map `PDPT[8+]`; read RIP/CR2 if reachable, don't theorize blind. (b) `run: failed to launch background job` after `iam &` ⟹ #43 returned <0 (a from-disk ELF-load failure, distinct from the mmap fix). (c) FB freezes after `bye` ⟹ the reap-fence deadlock survived (the self-owned clause didn't fire). **On any falsify the fix is NOT byte-reverted** (QEMU-proven) — the burn tells us where iron diverges.
  - **Rides this same burn (no extra risk):** the **1.50.7 (a) bg-fault survival** iron-pending item below — optional: `faulter &` (a faulting bg job) → the box SURVIVES + prompt stays live. 1.50.8 waitpid#4 + 1.50.9 fence + 1.50.10 guards are otherwise pid-array/boot-context logic with no iron-only path beyond the agnsh-prompt proof above.

  **FLASH:** `build/agnos` = the BARE 1.50.10 kernel from `scripts/burn-prep.sh` (sweep-gated). `install-usb.sh --update` (ESP-only; agnos-fs survives). NO auto-run — user flashes + watches + reports ([[feedback_iron_burns_block_other_work]]).

**1.50.7 — process-isolation hardening (bg-job fault teardown + real `proc_get_ppid`). QEMU-green; bite (a) bg-fault survival is the one iron-pending item.** Two sovereign items from the 2026-06-29 kavach↔agnos compat audit. **(b) real `proc_get_ppid`** is fully QEMU-validated (hermetic `PPID_SELFTEST` → `ppid: child-gate PASS`; the kill#16 child-only gate is now real) — no iron dependency, it's pure pid-array logic. **(a) bg-job fault teardown** is the iron item:

- **Hypothesis (this burn):** a background (`&`) ring-3 proc that takes a CPL3 fault (`#PF`/`#GP`/`#UD`) no longer halts the box. The bg path in `fault_kill_current` is already torn down (`proc_set_state(pid,0)`+SIGCHLD); instead of falling through to the IDT stub's canary-bar halt it now `sti;hlt`-loops, and the next timer tick's `do_context_switch` saves the dead slot (harmless, state 0), declines to revive it, and switches to the next ready proc (agnsh / idle) via the timer ISR's own iretq epilogue — the proven preemption path, no new switch mechanism. preempt_count is 0 (only CPL3 faults reach this fn).
- **✅ PASS:** launch a faulting program as a bg job (`faulter &`) at the agnsh prompt → the box SURVIVES (no canary bar across the FB, no hang), the prompt stays live, and the bg slot reaps (SIGCHLD to its real parent = agnsh, now that ppid is real). The FOREGROUND fault path is unchanged and already QEMU-green (`fault-kill-smoke: PASS` — `run: exit 142` + `SURVIVED`).
- **❌ FALSIFIES → NEXT:** the canary bar paints + the box halts on a bg-job fault despite the `sti;hlt` yield ⟹ either (i) the timer isn't preempting in that context (capture whether a tick fires — the dead proc's CR3 maps the ISR via the PD[0..127] kernel mirror, so a no-switch would point at `sched_active`/`proc_count<2`), or (ii) `sched_next` finds nothing ready (idle proc 1 should always be ready) — read RIP/regs from the QEMU monitor, do not theorize blind ([[feedback_disassemble_before_theorizing_hangs]]). A deterministic bg-fault selftest (spawn `/bin/faulter` via `spawn_path`#43 + assert survival across a context switch) is deferred — heavier than a hermetic test; this burn is its first real exercise. NO auto-run per [[feedback_iron_burns_block_other_work]].

**1.50.8 — waitpid#4 ownership gate (rides this same burn; NO iron-only risk).** The 1.50.7 real `proc_get_ppid` un-blocked the one waitpid#4 item from the 1.44.22 multi-threading cleanup (the row's other three: `sched_next` single-pass already done 1.47.8; MADT-AP-enum + loader-DRY never ppid-blocked, stay deferred). `waitpid#4` gained an ownership gate (`proc_may_reap`: init reaps anyone, a non-init caller reaps only its direct child) so a non-parent can't steal a child's exit code + collapse the slot. **Fully QEMU-proven, no iron dependency** — unlike the bg-fault teardown above, this is behavior-preserving pid-array logic with no iron-only path: `ring3-smoke` `parent spawn+wait OK` (spawn#3 + waitpid#4, a parent reaping its own child = the gate's happy path) PASSES, and the extended `PPID_SELFTEST` (`ppid: child-gate PASS`) covers `proc_may_reap`'s truth table. The agnsh-bg `&` end-to-end consumer is blocked by the **pre-existing `spawn_path#43` breakage** (the bg job never launches — upstream of the reap; same baseline failure as exec-smoke's `spawnpath#43`, NOT this change). Nothing extra to watch for on the burn beyond the 1.50.7 bg-fault hypothesis above.

> **✅✅✅ mmap-ARENA FULL LIFT IRON-VALIDATED 2026-06-29 (1.50.2 — TAGGED + RELEASED).** Burn built `MMAP_HIMEM_E2E_SELFTEST=1` on the default-on 1.50.2 kernel; the FB shows **`mmap-himem-e2e: >1GB map+free PASS`** on real 64 GB Zen (NVMe+1.9 TB-AHCI box, DHCP `192.168.1.193`, full render to `[ASSIST] >`). That self-test maps a **1.026 GB CONTIGUOUS** high-arena region (513 × 2 MB at 128 GB) via `proc_map_page_hi` — spanning PDPT[128]+[129], on-demand per-proc PD alloc ACROSS a PDPT boundary — then tears the address space down and asserts the 2 MB free-count is fully restored. So on real silicon: the ~768 MB single-process mmap cap is lifted (>1 GB works), the high-VA arena backed by the direct-map is reachable, and `proc_free_address_space`'s PDPT[128..511] sweep frees it with no leak — **all under the default-on boot-CR3 switch + the `vmm_remap_uc_2mb` guard** (the full boot renders). **1.50.2 TAGGED + RELEASED.** Remaining (deferred follow-ons, not blocking): a *ring-3* userland >1 GB program (the selftest drives the mechanism directly), high-range `munmap` (returns -1 today; teardown reclaims at exit), per-proc cursor. [[feedback_top_level_photos_are_fresh_iron]]

> **✅✅ DEFAULT-ON SOAK BURN PASSED 2026-06-29 (1.50.1, second clean iron burn — the one that gates the next RAM items).** Built `PDPT_GUARD_SELFTEST=1` on the **default-on** 1.50.1 kernel (no switch flag — it ships on now). Full-render boot to `[ASSIST] >` on real Zen with a MORE demanding disk topology than burn 1: **NVMe primary + a ~1.9 TB AHCI SATA secondary** (`ahci: port 0 LBA48=3907029168 sectors`), a 3-partition GPT (ESP 255 MiB + agnos-fs 25 GB + a 512 MiB `MSFT Basic fstest`), so AHCI/NVMe/xHCI/r8169 are ALL live in PDPT[3] beside the framebuffer — and it renders clean (ext2+jbd2 seq=174 + FAT mounted, SMP-4, DHCP `192.168.1.192`, `net: L2 OK`, `kybernet: 16177228 free pages` ≈ 62 GB). `pdpt-guard PASS` + `kernel CR3: own PML4` scrolled off-top (both print before `dev_init`). **The default-on switch + the guard fix are now doubly iron-confirmed** (gated burn 1 + default-on soak). **CLEARED to proceed to the next RAM items (user mmap-arena).** [[feedback_top_level_photos_are_fresh_iron]]

> **✅ BITE-2 IRON BURN PASSED 2026-06-29 (top-level photo — full-render boot to the agnsh prompt on real 64 GB Zen).** Built `BOOTCR3_OWN_PML4=1` (the user skipped `PDPT_GUARD_SELFTEST`, so no `pdpt-guard` canary line — the guard FIX is unconditional, so it was active regardless). The FB renders the ENTIRE post-`dev_init` boot — ext2/jbd2/FAT mounted, `smp: cpus online: 4`, DHCP real lease `192.168.1.192` + `arp REPLY gw_mac=d4:6a:91:ce:70:60` + `net: L2 OK`, `kybernet: 16177229 free pages` (~62 GB) — then `[ASSIST] >` and the user typed **`iam`** which ran and reported `Memory: 61 GiB`. **This is the decisive iron proof:** (a) the FB did NOT freeze → the `vmm_remap_uc_2mb` guard fix HELD on real silicon (FB stayed WC while NVMe/AHCI/xHCI/net all UC-remapped into the SAME PDPT[3] 1 GB entry — the exact clobber the pre-burn review predicted); (b) the **keyboard works** (`iam` typed + executed) → the xHCI BAR also survived (falsify branch B did not fire); (c) >256 MB RAM is fully usable under the switch (62 GB free, userland sees 61 GiB). The explicit `kernel CR3: own PML4 (0x1000)` line scrolled off the top (it prints right after the FB WC remap), but the flag was set + the switch is QEMU-proven to execute, and the device-remaps-coexisting-with-live-FB-and-keyboard is the behavior only the *active* switch + guard fix produce. **Bite 2 CLOSED on iron.** Next: promote the switch default-on (flip the gate) and/or 1.50.1 (user mmap-arena). [[feedback_top_level_photos_are_fresh_iron]] [[feedback_fb_logs_over_cmos]]

> **Where we were (pre-burn)**: the 1.49.x >256 MB RAM arc is iron-closed ([`#tracker-149x-cycle`](#tracker-149x-cycle)). 1.50.0 lands two foundation bites toward making that RAM *fully usable*; both QEMU-green. Bite 1 needs no iron burn (a self-test + a corruption fix, proven at `-m 32G`). **Bite 2 (the boot-CR3 switch) was the iron item this cycle — now PASSED.**

**BITE 1 — full-RAM stress + PCI-hole gap fix (QEMU-validated, no iron burn needed).** `PMM_RAMSTRESS_SELFTEST` (`pmm.cyr pmm_ramstress_run`) allocs thousands of 2 MB regions across the machine, sentinels each through the direct-map under CR3 0x1000, verifies + frees. It caught a real latent corruption: `pmm_migrate_bitmap` reserved non-conventional memmap *entries* but not *gaps*, so the **2–4 GB PCI hole** (absent from the UEFI memmap) stayed free → `pmm_alloc_2mb` handed out unbacked hole regions → direct-map access hit MMIO (`-m 32G`: `bad=1770 firstbad_phys=0x81000000`). FIXED: above 256 MB reserve-by-default + free only `EfiConventionalMemory` (type 7) — re-stress `-m 32G` `regions=16354 bad=0`. (Latent follow-ons, logged-not-silent: `free_count` cosmetically over-counts reserved gaps; `pmm_alloc_2mb` bottom-up scan is O(n²) with no next-free hint.)

**BITE 2 HYPOTHESIS (boot-CR3 → own PML4):** the kernel currently runs boot/idle on gnoboot's *transient* boot CR3 (`0x3fc01000`); only per-proc CR3s + kernel threads carry the direct-map (the 1.49.9 misdiagnosis root). Switching the boot/idle context onto its OWN complete `0x1000` PML4 — built by `pt_init`, carrying the direct-map from `pmm_setup_directmap` — closes that class structurally and lets the boot/idle context reach >256 MB directly. The switch is placed **late** (`main.cyr`, after the post-pmm FB WC remap, before `dev_init`) so every gnoboot-CR3-relative access is already done: the UEFI memmap (top of RAM) was read by `pmm_probe/extend/migrate/setup_directmap` under gnoboot's full-identity CR3; `boot_info` is already in the low `boot_info_copy`; the FB was just remapped **WC** (the earlier early-switch attempt left it a 1 GB **WB** huge page under `0x1000` → writes cached, never reached the display → a "freeze at Page tables: 1024MB mapped" *mirage* while serial showed the full boot); `dev_init` runs after → maps device BARs into the now-active `0x1000`.

**BITE 2 EXPECTATIONS (read the FB photo — [[feedback_fb_logs_over_cmos]]):** built with `BOOTCR3_OWN_PML4=1`, boots to the agnsh `[ASSIST] >` prompt with the FB rendering the WHOLE boot (the `kernel CR3: own PML4 (0x1000)` line near the top, then NVMe/AHCI/GPT/VFS/ext2/heap/scheduler/kybernet/agnsh — all of which run *after* the switch). On archaemenid the FB BAR sits in the 32-bit hole (<1 GB → `pt_init` maps it in a 2 MB-page PD, already WC from the early line-25 remap), so the FB-WC concern is the *easier* case than the QEMU q35 FB-at-2.26 GB-WB case that already passed.

**BITE 2 FALSIFY → NEXT:** (a) freeze/blank FB right after `Page tables: …MB mapped` with serial (if available) still advancing ⟹ the FB is still WB under `0x1000` on real Zen (the WC remap didn't take) — capture whether the FB BAR is <1 GB or high. (b) #PF/halt after the switch during `dev_init` ⟹ a device BAR ≥4 GB the post-switch remap didn't place in `0x1000` — capture CR2/RIP. (c) boots but a later >256 MB op faults ⟹ a directmap-inheritance gap distinct from the per-proc path. **On any falsify, the switch is GATED OFF by default** — production builds are byte-unaffected, so a failed promotion burn does NOT cost boot-to-shell; just don't pass the flag.

**BITE 2 PRE-BURN ADVERSARIAL REVIEW (2026-06-29) — caught a REAL burn-blocker QEMU structurally cannot surface.** A 6-lens / 25-agent refute-by-default review (the discipline the 1.46.x SMP arc used before every burn) found that **`vmm_remap_uc_2mb` lacks the idempotency guard its WC twin `vmm_remap_wc_2mb` has** (`kernel/core/vmm.cyr`). On iron the GOP framebuffer (`~0xd0000000`, PDPT[3]) shares ONE 1 GB PDPT entry with EVERY device BAR (NVMe `0xfcd…`/AHCI `0xfcc…`/xHCI `0xfc8/9…`/r8169 `0xfcf…`/IOMMU `0xfeb…`, ALL in PDPT[3] — confirmed from this box's sysfs; ZERO BARs ≥4 GB). So once the kernel runs on `0x1000`, the first device's UC remap **after** the line-243 FB WC remap re-shatters PDPT[3] and reverts the FB to WB → **frozen display = blinded burn**. QEMU can't see it (its FB is PDPT[2], lone NVMe PDPT[3] — never the same entry), which is exactly why the first QEMU pass looked clean. **FIXED**: added the idempotency guard to `vmm_remap_uc_2mb` (mirror of the WC twin; reuse the shattered PD + edit only the target PDE to UC). A new gated `PDPT_GUARD_SELFTEST` forces the same-PDPT collision synthetically (PDPT[4]) and proved it: **`pdpt-guard FAIL` pre-fix → `pdpt-guard PASS` post-fix** at `-m 8G`. The parallel agnos session independently hardened ACPI firmware-pointer reads for the same switch (`acpi.cyr acpi_va` — direct-map-aware ≥4 GB tables), covering the review's "post-switch high-phys reads" lens.

**BITE 2 QEMU VALIDATION (GREEN, GENUINE — [[feedback_qemu_test_agnos_userland]] [[feedback_restage_disk_before_boot]]):** with the guard fix + a **forced fresh disk stage**, `-m 8G` (q35/KVM/-smp 4/NVMe) serial shows `kernel CR3: own PML4 (0x1000)` (switch executes) + `pdpt-guard PASS` (FB-clobber fixed) + reaches `[ASSIST] >` at `2mb_top_region=5120`; a QMP framebuffer screendump (`build/agnsh-smoke/bootcr3-8g.png`) renders the entire post-switch boot. Production build (gate off) unregressed: `check.sh` 11/11, `agnsh-smoke` PASS. **PROCESS NOTE:** the FIRST "QEMU-validated" pass was HOLLOW — manual boots reused a STALE staged disk so the switch was never in the booted kernel; a side-effect probe after a forced re-stage exposed it ([[feedback_restage_disk_before_boot]]). The earlier "switch doesn't execute / cyrius drops top-level statements" trail was entirely that stale-disk artifact; cyrius is exonerated.

**NEXT (this cycle):** promote bite 2 (flagged iron burn → flip default-on) ; then **1.50.1 = user mmap-arena expansion** (the per-proc arena is ONE PD = ~1 GB, `[0x10000000, 0x40000000)`; lift it so a single process can heap past 1 GB). Deferred to bigger hardware: the >64 GB multi-region contiguous bitmap. NO auto-run per [[feedback_iron_burns_block_other_work]] — the user flashes + burns.

### Tracker: 1.49.x cycle — full-RAM extension (>256 MB RAM) on real 64 GB Zen: dynamic RAM-backed PMM bitmap + the >256 MB kernel direct-map (✅ **ARC IRON-VALIDATED + CLOSED 2026-06-28** — BURN 1 (1.49.11) RAM-init-✅'d but locked up launching agnsh → 3 raw-phys >256 MB sites, FIXED 1.49.12; **RE-BURN (1.49.12) PASSED on real 64 GB Zen — agnsh launches, >256 MB RAM usable** (user verbal report). Also cleared the unburned 1.47.x fault-resilience+perf+KASLR + 1.48.x FAT/exFAT-perf that rode along — prior burn was 1.46.8. **NEXT: the 1.50.x RAM-continuation arc.**). {#tracker-149x-cycle}

> **✅ RE-BURN (1.49.12) PASSED 2026-06-28 (user verbal report — no FB photo for the pass).** The 1.49.12 raw-phys fix landed and the box boots to the agnsh prompt on real 64 GB Zen with the full RAM online — closing the 1.49.x full-RAM arc on iron AND, in the same artifact, the long-pending 1.47.x (proc-teardown-on-fault, per-process fd tables, ext2/FAT perf incl. the first NVMe >2-page PRP-list DMA on iron, full-binary KASLR) + 1.48.x (FAT/exFAT perf) work that hadn't burned since 1.46.8. Arc closed; the 1.50.x RAM-continuation arc opens ([`#tracker-150x-cycle`](#tracker-150x-cycle)).

**★ BURN 1 RESULT (2026-06-28, photo [`iron-nuc-zen-photos/14911-agnos-1.49.11-burn1-lockup-launching-agnsh.jpg`](../../iron-nuc-zen-photos/14911-agnos-1.49.11-burn1-lockup-launching-agnsh.jpg)) — the RAM extension WORKS; a latent raw-phys bug it exposed did not.** The FB shows a FULLY healthy kernel init: **`kybernet: 16177224 free pages`** (≈ 62 GB of a 64 GB box — the dynamic RAM-backed bitmap migration ran and the PMM tracks the whole machine, vs the old 256 MB), `smp: cpus online: 4`, DHCP/ARP/`net: L2 OK`, ext2+jbd2+FAT all mounted — then a HARD LOCKUP **immediately after `kybernet: exec /bin/agnsh`** (no fault bar rendered). **Reproduced in QEMU at `-m 8G`** (passes at `-m 512M`) → RAM-size-dependent, NOT iron-only. QMP `info registers` at the hang: **`RIP` in the kpanic halt loop, `HLT=1`, `IF=0`, `CR2≈0x174000000` (~6.2 GB), `RDX=0x200000`** = a 2 MB memory op faulting at a multi-GB *raw-physical* address. **Root cause:** 1.49.9 added `pmm_kva_for_access` (identity ≤256 MB / direct-map above) and fixed `elf_load_from_file`, but **three sibling sites still touched freshly-allocated 2 MB pages by their raw phys as an identity VA** — `sys_mmap`'s zero-fill, and `elf_load`'s segment copy + stack `vmm_map`. Latent while `pmm_alloc_2mb` was capped at 256 MB; **1.49.11's cap lift let a >256 MB region reach them → #PF at a >256 MB identity VA (unmapped) → kpanic-halt.** agnsh itself loads fine (via the already-correct `elf_load_from_file`); it locks up when it enters ring 3 and its **first heap `mmap` hits `sys_mmap`**. **FIX (1.49.12):** route all three through `pmm_kva_for_access` (the user PTE still gets raw `phys` via `proc_map_page`; only the kernel's transient zero/copy uses the handle). **Verified in QEMU:** `-m 8G` + `-m 32G` now reach the agnsh prompt (`[ASSIST] >`); `-m 512M` baseline unaffected; `agnsh-smoke` / `mmap-smoke` 2/2 / `exec-smoke` / `ram-probe-smoke` / `check.sh` 11/11 all green. **RE-BURN (1.49.12) confirms agnsh launches on real 64 GB Zen.** Lesson: QEMU at large `-m` (8 G+) reproduces the >256 MB allocation paths — add it to the pre-burn gate, since the default smokes run ≤1 GB and never cross 256 MB. [[feedback_qemu_test_agnos_userland]] [[feedback_disassemble_before_theorizing_hangs]]

**Context — the 1.49.9 "cyrius high-VA blocker" was a MISDIAGNOSIS, now corrected.** The 1.49.x kernel-capability-gaps arc's full-RAM-init item shipped >256 MB RAM across 1.49.6–1.49.11. 1.49.9 *thought* the >256 MB path was blocked because `store64`/`load64` to the 8 GB direct-map VA returned 0 with no fault — and blamed cyrius. **1.49.10 root-caused it:** the probe ran under gnoboot's **TRANSIENT boot CR3** (`0x3fc01000`, whose `PDPT[8]` is a 1 GB *identity* huge page to unbacked phys 8 GB → reads 0, no fault), NOT the kernel's `0x1000` PML4 where the direct-map lives; the bite-2 `directmap: low+hi OK` probe was a two-zeros false match. Under **CR3 `0x1000`** (where kernel threads + every per-proc CR3 run) the SAME store/load round-trips perfectly → **cyrius EXONERATED** (issue doc updated; `agnos/docs/development/issue/2026-06-28-cyrius-high-va-load-store.md`). The boot probe was rewritten to validate a true >256 MB VA under CR3 0x1000. **1.49.11 brought >256 MB RAM online** via a dynamic RAM-backed PMM bitmap (`pmm_migrate_bitmap`) + lifting `pmm_alloc_2mb`'s 256 MB cap. [[project_agnos_directmap_placement]].

**HYPOTHESIS (>256 MB RAM on iron):** on real 60 GB Zen, the boot probe reads the UEFI memmap; `pmm_migrate_bitmap` swaps the 256 MB bootstrap bitmap for a RAM-backed one (one 2 MB region) covering the full 60 GB (< the 64 GB single-bitmap ceiling, so NOT capped); `pmm_alloc_2mb` hands out 2 MB user pages above the 256 MB identity ceiling, zeroed/loaded via `DIRECTMAP_BASE + phys` under the per-proc CR3; and the always-on boot probe confirms the direct-map under CR3 0x1000. Everything QEMU-proven at `-m 1024M`; this burn is the first real-hardware exercise at 60 GB + against the real UEFI memmap shape.

**EXPECTATIONS (read the FB photo, never CMOS — [[feedback_fb_logs_over_cmos]]):** boots to the agnsh prompt + (a) `RAM: usable=~60000MB top=…` (scales to real RAM, not the old hardcode); (b) `PMM: 2mb_top_region≈30720` (60 GB / 2 MB; the migration lifted it from the bootstrap 128); (c) `directmap: >256MB OK (kernel CR3)` — the always-on probe round-tripped a true >256 MB page through the direct-map (**or** `directmap: installed` — the no-CR3-switch structural fallback, which fires if gnoboot's boot `RSP ≥ 256 MB`); (d) commands work (also exercising the unburned 1.47.x faulting-proc-kill-returns-to-prompt + 1.48.x FAT/exFAT perf + the `KASLR: kernel_base=<hex>` in-window line).

**FALSIFY → NEXT:** (a) `RAM: usable=` ≤256 MB or doesn't scale ⟹ the memmap parse mis-reads real UEFI entries (descriptor stride / the type==7 conventional filter) — capture the reported MB + memmap shape. (b) `directmap: >256MB FAIL` ⟹ the direct-map doesn't translate under CR3 0x1000 on real Zen despite QEMU-green — next-suspect = a real-Zen paging-structure-cache/TLB subtlety the migration's CR3 reload misses, OR a 60 GB-memmap hole the migration's non-conventional reservation didn't cover (capture which). (c) **fault/halt right after `PMM: 2mb_top_region=`** ⟹ the always-on CR3-switch probe (`dm_roundtrip_kernel_cr3` flips CR3→0x1000 + back at boot — the FIRST iron exercise of a boot-time CR3 switch) faulted because the boot stack/code isn't mapped under 0x1000 on real Zen; the `RSP<256 MB` gate should prevent it (falls back to the no-switch `directmap: installed` path) — if it fires anyway, capture RSP + the fault vector. (d) a >256 MB user allocation faults during real use ⟹ a per-proc-CR3 direct-map-inheritance gap that only shows at 60 GB scale.

**Validation gates (QEMU — all GREEN before the burn, per [[feedback_qemu_test_agnos_userland]]):** `ram-probe-smoke` 4/4 (incl. the corrected >256 MB round-trip under kernel CR3 at `-m 1024M`), `pmm-fullram-smoke` 4/4, the gated `PMM_HIRAM_SELFTEST` end-to-end (`2mb_top_region=511`, allocates a region at phys 256 MB, round-trips `f00dcafe` through the direct-map), `mmap-smoke` 2/2 (the stale 16 MB → 64 GB out-of-range test fix), `agnsh-smoke`, `exec-smoke` (built with `EXEC_SELFTEST=1 EXT2_WRITE_SELFTEST=1`), `check.sh` 11/11. The HIRAM/PMM selftests are gated OFF in the production burn artifact (verified: 0 `HIRAM:` strings in `build/agnos`).

**KILL-SWITCH:** the >256 MB path activates only when RAM>256 MB — a ≤256 MB boot keeps the bootstrap bitmap byte-identical to 1.49.8. The boot probe's CR3 switch is gated `RAM>320 MB && RSP<256 MB` and degrades to a non-switching structural check.

**NEXT STAGES → 1.50.x if the burn is clear (user 2026-06-28):** the >64 GB multi-region contiguous bitmap (one 2 MB bitmap caps coverage at 64 GB) + the boot-CR3 → own-complete-PML4 cleanup (so the boot CR3 also carries the full kernel map — the proper fix for the "boot CR3 can't see kernel VM edits" class that caused the 1.49.9 misdiagnosis; needs `0x1000`'s PDPT completed for the FB/device BARs first). **1.50.x is now the RAM-continuation slot** (roadmap updated 2026-06-28); the agnos-2.0 rewrite slid further — it's cyrius-gated on **native generics** (unpinned in cyrius `roadmap-future.md`), so it's not schedulable until that lands. NO auto-run per [[feedback_iron_burns_block_other_work]] — the user flashes + burns.

### Tracker: 1.47.x cycle — fault-resilience first (proc-teardown-on-fault → per-process fd tables), then perf + KASLR (OPEN 2026-06-27). {#tracker-147x-cycle}

**ALL the fault/proc-teardown engineering rides under 1.47.0** — no tag pushed, so the open version absorbs the work; the **test burn comes after the work is complete**, and a patch cut happens only if the burn surfaces issues (then perf). The arc-open commit itself was just the banner + docs. Theme set by the user 2026-06-27 (blend): the just-closed 1.46.x SMP arc surfaced two systemic robustness gaps that LEAD this arc, with the originally-slotted perf tail folding in after.

**Bite ladder (sequence, not commitments):**
1. **proc-teardown-on-fault (headline).** Today an unhandled **ring-3** `#PF`/`#GP`/`#UD` lands in the `idt.cyr` fault handler and HALTS the box behind the 1.45.16 fault-canary bar (e.g. the cyrius-doom CPL3 `#PF`, CR2=0x120b3740 / RIP=0x45ff1d, on the 1.46.8 burn — [[project_doom_on_agnos_blockers]]). Target: a CPL3 fault TERMINATES the faulting proc (mark dead → free its AS + slot) and returns control to the agnsh prompt. A userland segfault must not take the system down; there is **no signal-delivery / proc-teardown-on-fault path yet**.
2. **per-process fd tables.** Replace the global `vfs_table[]` (which the pipe redirect swaps) with per-proc fd tables → streaming / SMP-safe / N-stage pipes (filed by the 1.46.11 pipe work, `…-pipes-per-proc-fd-streaming.md`).
3. **Arc tail (originally-slotted 1.47.x):** the 1.42.x Track-A perf remainder (scheduler/context-switch, syscall entry/exit, block-IO batching, ext2/4 hot loops) + full-binary KASLR Option A (PIE-unblocked at cyrius v6.1.41).

**HYPOTHESIS (bite 1 — proc-teardown-on-fault):** the `#PF`/`#GP`/`#UD` handlers can distinguish a **CPL3** fault (saved-frame CS RPL=3, equivalently the `#PF` `err` U/S bit) from a CPL0 fault; on a CPL3 fault the kernel runs the existing `proc_reap` / AS-teardown for the *current* proc and re-enters the scheduler (which returns to agnsh, the parent foreground proc) WITHOUT corrupting kernel state — because the fault is taken on the proc's own RSP0 kstack (1.46.x per-proc stacks) and its AS is already isolated. **FALSIFY → NEXT:** (a) the teardown itself faults (→ `#DF`) ⟹ it touches freed/unmapped state mid-path ⟹ defer the free: just mark-dead + scheduler-skip, free lazily in the reaper. (b) the box still halts (canary bar) on a deliberate ring-3 fault ⟹ the CPL0/CPL3 discrimination is wrong or the handler still calls `kpanic` for CPL3 ⟹ read the FB photo, decode the saved-frame CS/err. (c) agnsh never regains the prompt after the kill ⟹ the scheduler resumed a dead/wrong proc ⟹ verify the foreground/parent resume target. **Do NOT** weaken the CPL0 path — a *kernel* fault must still halt loudly.

**Validation gates (QEMU before any burn, per [[feedback_qemu_test_agnos_userland]]):** a new `fault-kill-smoke` — exec a ring-3 program that deliberately dereferences an unmapped address; assert the kernel emits a `killed: pid N #PF @CR2=…` line and the agnsh prompt RETURNS + accepts the next command (not a halt). Plus `check.sh` 11/11 + agnsh-smoke + the exec/ring3 smokes (no regression). **PASS (iron):** boot-to-shell, run a known-faulting tool (or cyrius-doom into its sustained-play OOB), and the shell SURVIVES with a kill message instead of the canary-bar halt. **Read the FB photo, never CMOS** ([[feedback_fb_logs_over_cmos]]). No burn until QEMU-green + the user stages it.

**✅ BITE 1 (proc-teardown-on-fault) QEMU-GREEN 2026-06-27 — landed under 1.47.0, iron-burn pending.** Impl: a CPL3 privilege gate in the `#PF`/`#GP`/`#UD` exception stubs (`idt.cyr`) → `fault_kill_current` (`syscall.cyr`, mirrors `exit#0` + `kernel_resume`), wired via the `exc_ring3_kill_fn` indirect hook (set in `main.cyr`). `scripts/fault-kill-smoke.sh` (the `FAULT_SELFTEST` kernel runs a ring-3 ELF that reads an unmapped 5 GB addr): **`run: exit 142` (128+14) + `fault: SURVIVED back in kernel`** — the box did NOT halt. No regression: `exec-smoke` 16/16 (the shared `kernel_resume` / execwait#37 path), `check.sh` 11/11. CPL0 (kernel) faults still canary-halt by construction (the gate's `jne` only diverts CPL3). Scope = foreground exec child (the cyrius-doom case); bg-job fault teardown is a follow-on. The iron burn confirms the #37 agnsh→tool path on real Zen — to be STAGED BY THE USER (no tag until then). per-process fd tables (bite 2) + perf/KASLR (tail) still ahead — all under 1.47.0.

**✅ BITE 2 (per-process fd tables) QEMU-GREEN 2026-06-27 — landed under 1.47.0 across 2a/2b/2c, iron-burn pending.** Replaces the single global `vfs_table` (pipe redirect swapped it) with a per-proc table → the prerequisite for SMP-safe / streaming / N-stage pipes. **2a** seam (`vfs_fd_table()` accessor over ~30 sites, byte-identical). **2b** per-proc storage (`proc_fd_base[16]`) + fork-like inheritance at `proc_create_user` + free-at-reap + the execwait#37 redirect retargeted to the CHILD's table; fallback-to-global keeps un-hooked paths safe. **2c** owner-keyed pipe refcount (`pipe_rc_*`) + the `VFS_PIPE` arm in `vfs_close` → frees the shared 4 KB buffer on the owner's last close (kills the per-pipeline leak); a child closing an inherited copy is a no-op. QEMU: `check.sh` 11/11 · **pipe-smoke PASS** (the real inheritance + redirect-to-child + shared-buffer drain test) · exec-smoke 15/15 + fault-kill-smoke (bite 1 still green under per-proc tables) · new **`PIPE_RC_SELFTEST`** = `piperc: buffer FREED OK` (heap_frees witness; freed on the LAST close, not the first) + `double-close safe`. Iron burn (bite 1 + bite 2 together) staged by the user. NEXT in the arc: the perf tail (scheduler/context-switch, syscall entry/exit, block-IO batching, ext2/4 hot loops) + full-binary KASLR Option A.

**✅ PERF PATCHES + FULL-BINARY KASLR — QEMU-GREEN 2026-06-27, iron rides the 1.47.x burn.** Perf: **1.47.1** ext2 block-write batching (`block_write_amp` multi=1/single=8), **1.47.2** ext2 dir-lookup inode reuse (`dirlk reuse=0 reread=1`). **KASLR — 1.47.3 (foundation) + 1.47.4 (full, Option A); pairs with gnoboot 0.6.0:** the kernel binary's physical load base is randomized per boot — build `CYRIUS_PIE=1` → relocation-free ET_DYN / RIP-relative kernel; gnoboot picks an RDRAND-slid, 2 MB-aligned base in [32 MB, 254 MB) + jumps to base+e_entry. The slide sits above the 16 MB PMM ceiling + below the 256 MB per-proc-CR3 identity window → **no kernel mem-rework, no relocation walk** (the "cyrius has no PIE mode" blocker was a stale-doc misread — cyrius PIE shipped v6.1.6/6.1.8, relocation-free). **QEMU:** `kaslr-smoke.sh` two-boot base-diff PASS (distinct in-window aligned bases: 0x2C00000 / 0x5200000 / 0x9400000 / 0xD000000 / 0xA600000…), exec-smoke 15/15 on a slid kernel (per-proc CR3 maps it), fault-kill + dirlk + `check.sh` 11/11; non-PIE ET_EXEC unaffected (fixed 0x100000). **THE BURN (KASLR, user-staged):** boot the `CYRIUS_PIE` kernel on Zen → FB shows `KASLR: kernel_base=<hex>` in [0x2000000, 0xFE00000) (NOT 0x100000), boot reaches the agnsh prompt, exec works. **FALSIFY → NEXT:** (a) `kernel_base=100000` ⟹ firmware reserved all 16 re-rolled slots → the fallback fired (benign; investigate why real UEFI denies [32MB,254MB) on this box — likely a memmap-type filter; widen/relocate the window). (b) fault/halt right after the `KASLR:` line ⟹ the slid image isn't mapped under some CR3 on real Zen (read the FB fault bar) — next-suspect = a CR3 whose identity window < the slid base; capture base + fault vector. **KILL-SWITCH:** ship the non-PIE ET_EXEC kernel — KASLR is opt-in at build time (`CYRIUS_PIE`), so KASLR-off = build without it (fixed 0x100000, iron-proven).

**✅ PERF PATCHES 1.47.5 + 1.47.6 + 1.47.7 (ext2 read+write paths) — QEMU-GREEN 2026-06-27.** **1.47.5** single-indirect `l1` reuse (`ext2_read_at` caches l1 across a sequential read; `indirect reuse=1 reread=2`). **1.47.6** multi-block read coalescing — `ext2_read_at` coalesces physically-contiguous block runs into ONE direct multi-LBA NVMe DMA (≤16 blocks / 64 KB) straight into a DMA-safe dest (page-aligned + in the 0-256 MB identity window; gated `iommu_active==0`, Intel VT-d → bounce path), no scratch / no PMM cost; `block_coalesce_amp multi=1 perblk=16`, exec-smoke 15/15, e2fsck-clean. **⚠ IRON-DMA WATCH (1.47.6):** this is the FIRST ext2-read use of the NVMe **>2-page PRP-list** path (16 pages → 15 list entries); QEMU's NVMe model can't validate real-NVMe + real-PRP DMA. **THE BURN:** boot-to-shell + run a big binary (`doom` / a large kriya applet) on Zen → it must exec + read correctly (no garbled binary, no #PF reading the ELF). **FALSIFY → NEXT:** a big binary faults/garbles where small ones work ⟹ the >2-page PRP-list DMA misbehaves on real NVMe ⟹ **flip the kill-switch `ext2_read_coalesce_max=1`** (forces single-block = the iron-proven path) + investigate the PRP-list build / IOMMU state. **KILL-SWITCH:** `ext2_read_coalesce_max=1` (one-liner) reverts 1.47.6 to per-block; reads < 2 blocks never coalesce regardless. **1.47.7** multi-block WRITE coalescing (write mirror of 1.47.6) — `ext2_write_at` coalesces contiguous full-block writes from a DMA-safe src into ONE direct multi-LBA NVMe command (skips the per-block copy + bounce; non-coalesced fallback now word-wide memcpy/memset); `block_coalesce_write_amp multi=1 perblk=16` + **`ext2w: write-coalesce 32KB round-trip OK`** (data read back byte-identical), e2fsck-clean. **⚠ The IRON-DMA WATCH now covers WRITES too** (same NVMe >2-page PRP-list path, opcode 0x01). **THE WRITE BURN:** a big-file WRITE on Zen (install / a large `cp`) then read back must match — FALSIFY = written data corrupt/garbled ⟹ the SAME shared kill-switch `ext2_read_coalesce_max=1` reverts BOTH read + write coalescing to per-block.

**⚠ ONE NEW FINDING (separate from the SMP PASS) — cyrius-doom sustained-play kernel fault.** The user launched **cyrius-doom 0.30.2** from agnsh; it renders + **plays**, but after **~1-2 minutes of active gameplay** it LOCKS UP with a **blue bar across the top** = the 1.45.16 `idt.cyr` CPU-exception fault-canary (`eax=0x000000FF` blue in BGRX, painted on ANY caught CPU exception) ⟹ this is a **CAUGHT KERNEL CPU EXCEPTION during sustained play, NOT a userland hang**. **The exact vector is UNKNOWN** (no FB photo, no CMOS `0x54` read of this fault yet — so per [[feedback_disassemble_before_theorizing_hangs]], the vector must be captured before the fix is theorized). DOOM is **non-MVP games-stable** ([[project_games_stable_ip_remakes]]) so this is a **finding, not a blocker** — the SMP STEP-2 PASS stands on its own (the user explicitly separated it: "all expected items ran without issue"). **🔬 VECTOR CAPTURED (CMOS `read-boot-log --verbose`, 2026-06-26) — it's a RING-3 #PF INSIDE DOOM, NOT a kernel/SMP fault.** `CMOS[0x54]=0x0e` = **vector 14 (#PF)**, fresh stamp (`0x55=0xe5`). `CR2=0x0000120b3740`, `err=0x04` = **not-present · read · USER(CPL3) · data**, faulting `RIP(low32)=0x0045ff1d`. Decode: DOOM is non-PIE (base `0x400000`, entry `0x400078`) so RIP maps directly to file VA `0x45ff1d`; the burned `build/doom_agnos` (Jun 19, == 0.30.2 HEAD which is Jun 14) disassembles there to `mov (%rax),%rax` where `rax = base + idx*8`, `base = *rcx` (a zone pointer landing ≈`0x12000000` = DOOM's mmap'd heap), `idx = [rbp-0x1e0]` — a textbook **Cyrius `base[idx]` 8-byte-element array read with `idx` OUT OF BOUNDS** (`idx*8 = CR2−base` overruns the mapped extent → read of unmapped page `0x120b3740`). Enclosing fn: prologue `~0x45efc7` (large ~488-byte frame), references `.bss` global `0x4844c0` shared by a sibling cluster (`0x45a38b/0x45a3f9/0x45b4d2/0x45c5a7/0x45ff27` = one DOOM module). **Conclusion: a USERLAND cyrius-doom OOB-array bug exposed during sustained gameplay (recent 0.30.x shooting/sprite paths) — agnos's guard-unmapped pages turn what on stock hardware would be a classic vanilla-DOOM static-limit overflow (vissprite/drawseg/visplane-class) into a clean #PF instead of silent corruption.** **NOT a STEP-2 regression** — the fault is at CPL3 (`err` U/S=1) and DOOM (a foreground proc) is BSP-pinned via the 1.46.6 `idle_owner`, so it is SMP-independent; the STEP-2 PASS above is unaffected.

  - **HYPOTHESIS (DOOM #PF):** cyrius-doom 0.30.2 indexes a zone-allocated array past its bounds during busy gameplay; the wild index resolves into an unmapped page in the mmap'd heap → ring-3 #PF. **Falsification path** (cyrius-doom-side, separate repo): rebuild `doom_agnos` UNSTRIPPED (or use a cyrius symbol map) to name the fn at `0x45efc7` + the array; reproduce in QEMU by scripting 1-2 min of busy play under `-d int` (a doom-play repro should reproduce the same `CR2≈0x12xxxxxx` / `RIP=0x45ff1d` #PF burn-free, per [[feedback_smp_step1_reproducible_in_qemu]]); bound/guard the index. If the QEMU repro does NOT fault but iron does, suspect an mmap-extent/zone-size mismatch in the agnos platform layer rather than a pure index bug.
  - **Two follow-ups, NEITHER blocks the SMP arc:** (1) **cyrius-doom** (games-stable, separate repo, [[project_games_stable_ip_remakes]]): the OOB `base[idx]` at fn `~0x45efc7` — the maintainer names + guards the array. (2) **agnos kernel-hardening** (server/desktop maturity, NOT MVP): an unhandled **ring-3** #PF currently halts/locks the box (canary bar + stop) instead of **terminating the faulting proc and returning to the agnsh prompt** — a userland segfault should not take the system down. This is the bigger systemic item the DOOM crash surfaced (no signal-delivery / proc-teardown-on-fault path yet); carry-forward.

Follow-up (2) (agnos ring-3-fault → proc-teardown) stays a carry-forward (server/desktop maturity, NOT MVP). **Post-SMP kernel cuts — all QEMU-validated, iron-PENDING (ride the next burn):** **1.46.9** `input_lock` (kb_buf SMP/ISR serialization — completes sub-bite 5), **1.46.10** exec argv cap 8→16 (`<tool> <sentence>` no longer drops past the 8th token), **1.46.11** `read#5` stdin-from-pipe → **two-stage shell pipes `cmd1 | cmd2`** (agnoshi 1.8.0 `sh_run_pipeline` + `pipe#25`/`exec_redirect#62`; QEMU `pipe-smoke` PASS — `owl -p /file | anuenue` truecolor-rainbows owl's stdout; store-and-forward, single-foreground, 4088-byte buffer; follow-ons → agnos `docs/development/issues/2026-06-27-agnos-pipe*`). anuenue 1.1.5 (truecolor on agnos). **Iron deploy:** re-stage `agnsh_agnos`(1.8.0)+`anuenue_agnos`(1.1.5) into `build/rootfs/bin` (`stage-agnsh.sh`/`stage-tools.sh`) then mount-copy onto the agnos-fs. Full detail → the agnos/agnoshi/anuenue/cyrius-doom CHANGELOGs.

**Arc hypotheses + falsification (write-before-burn; the engineering lands + QEMU-validates first, THEN a burn validates each):**

- **H1 — per-process kernel stacks unblock IF=1 preemptive `agnsh` on iron.** With each proc holding its own kernel stack + `RSP0`, re-enabling `exec_preempt` should let agnsh run preemptively (timer preempts it in ring 3 between prompt polls) and survive multi-command input on real Zen — the milestone 1.44.x DEFERRED (the iron shell currently runs cooperative IF=0 by deliberate gate, [[project_agnsh_if1_preempt_iron_blocker]]). **Falsifies** ⟹ freeze at/after the first preemptive timer tick (the 1.44.23 `RSP0`-in-live-`.bss` class, now structurally mitigated by per-proc kstacks) → NEXT: verify each proc's `RSP0` points into its OWN kstack VA (not the shared `0x3C0000`); bound the cross-privilege ISR + `do_context_switch` call-tree depth against the per-proc kstack size.
- **H2 — SMP-AP wake re-enabled (`smp_wake_enabled=1`) brings APs online on real Zen without hanging boot.** The 1.44.18 wake is QEMU-green (`smp-smoke` 3/3, `cpus online: 4`) but iron-UNPROVEN — the real-Zen INIT-SIPI timing is the flagged riskiest item and the wake is currently GATED OFF (1.44.25). **Falsifies** ⟹ boot hangs at AP wake / `cpus online` never reaches the MADT count → NEXT: the 1.44.21 bounded INIT/SIPI delivery-status polls (fail-safe `ICR_DELIVERY_WAIT`), MADT AP-enum (a 1.44.22 audit-deferred cleanup), per-Zen SIPI vector/delay tuning. (Smokes can't reach this — SMP boot-timing races `kybernet` under KVM, so the scheduler selftests are TCG-only by design; iron is where multi-core is actually proven.)
- **H3 — the single-core invariant unwinds cleanly.** The FS / shared-scratch-buffer code leans on single-threaded-single-core (no locking, shared buffers — [[project_multithreading_future_arc]]); per-proc kstacks + live APs must not corrupt those paths. **Falsifies** ⟹ FS corruption / torn shared-buffer state once a second core or a preempted-mid-FS proc is live → NEXT: audit `preempt_disable` gate coverage over every shared-scratch / non-reentrant FS path (the 1.44.1-2 reentrant-or-gated pattern), extend to real SMP locking where the gate alone is insufficient.

**First burn needs to show:** boot-to-shell on real Zen with `exec_preempt` re-enabled (preemptive agnsh, multi-command survives) AND `smp_wake_enabled=1` (`smp: cpus online: N` on the FB), full smoke battery green in QEMU first. **NO burn until the per-proc-kstack engineering lands + QEMU-validates.** Flash kernel via `install-usb.sh --update` (ESP); capture an FB photo per [[feedback_fb_logs_over_cmos]]; NO auto-run per [[feedback_iron_burns_block_other_work]].

**🛠 BITE 1 — per-process TSS.RSP0 kernel stacks — LANDED + QEMU-VALIDATED 2026-06-24 (iron-pending).** Design+adversarial review via a 9-agent workflow (5 surface-mappers → design → 3 adversarial reviewers); the review CAUGHT TWO would-be-triple-faults in the first draft and reshaped the bite:
- **Defect 1 (all 3 reviewers, critical): SYSCALL-stub `r10` clobber.** The draft routed a per-proc SYSCALL stack via an indirect load that clobbered `r10` = `&kernel_rsp_save` — which is read-through (`mov rdi,[r10]`) later with NO reload (in-code comment confirms it "MUST survive untouched") → garbage `user_rsp` to every syscall → triple-fault on the first syscall.
- **Defect 2 (regression lens, critical): pool collided with a live boot probe.** The draft put the kstack pool at region 8 (16 MB); `main.cyr:175 vmm_alloc_at(0x1000000)` 2 MB-remaps PD[8] in the boot CR3, destroying the pool's identity map under the exact CR3 idle/kthreads run under.
- **Convergent fix (adopted): scope bite 1 to TSS.RSP0-per-proc ONLY** (no syscall-stub change → no r10 risk; the shared syscall kstack + execwait second-stack stay byte-valid), and **place the pool at region 7 (14–16 MB = PD[7])** — top of the 0–16 MB boot identity map, supervisor-mapped under the boot CR3 AND every per-proc CR3 (which copies PD[0..127]), untouched by the PD[8] probe; reserved in `pmm_init`.
- **Implementation:** `proc_rsp0[16]` + `proc_rsp0_top` (0-fallback → legacy `0x3C0000`), assigned in `proc_alloc_slot`; `TSS.RSP0` set per-switch in `do_context_switch` (after `cr3_load`, APIC-read rule) + `sys_sched_yield` + `exec_and_wait`. The deep FS path stays on the unchanged syscall kstack; the RSP0 (interrupt) stack is shallow (ISR→ctxswitch), so 64 KB/proc is ample.
- **QEMU validation:** `ring3-smoke` **8/8** — IF=1 ring-3 procs take *real* CPL3→CPL0 timer preempts onto distinct region-7 RSP0 stacks (a misplaced/unmapped stack would `#DF` the preempt → this is the headline-capability proof, not just the legacy fallback); `agnsh-smoke` PASS (cooperative boot-to-shell, no regression); `thread-smoke` PASS (kthread preempt through the modified `do_context_switch`); `check.sh` **11/11**; `smp-smoke` = gated-config expected (AP wake still gated; boot continuity PASS); `readelf` image ends `0x2376a0` (2.3 MB, region 7 clear); `pmm` free pages 31215 (= −512, region-7 reservation confirmed). `build/agnos` **1,210,368 B**.
- **Iron expectation for bite 1 (when it rides a burn):** because RSP0 is consumed ONLY on a CPL3→CPL0 change and production agnsh launches **IF=0**, bite 1 is **INERT** on the current iron path — the burn must show **byte-identical boot-to-shell** vs the 1.45.17 baseline (proving the foundation is safe before bite 2 flips `exec_preempt=1`). **Falsification:** any boot regression vs 1.45.17 ⟹ the region-7 reservation or the per-switch `tss_set_rsp0` placement is wrong (the added per-switch APIC-ID read is the one new hardware access — the 1.44.18 "inert SMP change bisected to keyboard death" class — so a keyboard/boot anomaly points there first). **NO version tag until the arc proves out on iron** (user tags; [[feedback_no_unprompted_version_bumps]]).

**🛠 BITE 2 — IF=1 preemptive `agnsh` RE-ENABLED (`exec_preempt=1`, `kernel/user/init.cyr`) — LANDED + QEMU-VALIDATED 2026-06-24 (iron-pending; THIS is the bite the burn actually tests).** agnsh launches IF=1 again so the 100 Hz timer time-slices its prompt against `&` background jobs (`enter_ring3` consumes the flag → only agnsh's launch is preemptive; foreground execwait#37 stays IF=0). **This is the milestone 1.44.23 reverted:** at 1.44.23 the first CPL3→CPL0 timer tick took its ISR frame on the SHARED `RSP0=0x3C0000` which sat in **live kernel `.bss`** → triple-fault freeze at the banner (burns 1442x). **Bite 1 is the hypothesized fix** — RSP0 is now per-proc in region 7 (clear of `.bss`), so agnsh's first tick lands on agnsh's OWN stack. **QEMU validation:** `agnsh-bg-test` (sleeper & → prompt stays LIVE while the job runs, reaps `[1] Done`), `agnsh-multijob-test` (2 concurrent jobs, OUT-OF-ORDER reap, prompt live), `agnsh-smoke`, `check.sh` 11/11. `build/agnos` 1,210,384 B. **THE BURN (when the user runs it):** boot-to-shell on Zen → type multi-command → launch a `sleeper &` → expect the prompt stays live + the job reaps, NO banner freeze. **Falsification:** freeze at/after the banner (the 1.44.23 symptom) ⟹ region-7 RSP0 is NOT the (whole) fix — capture the FB photo; next-suspects = the per-switch APIC-read timing, or a deeper first-CPL3→CPL0-tick issue on Zen (read the FB bar = fault vs corner = spin per the 1.45.16 canary if re-armed). H1 (per-proc kstacks unblock IF=1 agnsh on iron) is settled by this burn.

**🔥 SMP SUB-BITE 7 STEP-1 — WAKE + AP-IDLE-ONLY — STAGED FOR BURN 2026-06-24 (flashed; awaiting user reboot).** The first iron test of the woken SMP path. **Flags:** `smp_wake_enabled=1` (main.cyr:2173) + `smp_sched_aps=0` (smp.cyr:145) → the BSP sends INIT-SIPI-SIPI to APs 1-3, each AP runs `ap_entry` (TPR=0, mask its LVT LINT0/LINT1, per-CPU SYSCALL MSRs, register its idle kthread, arm its LAPIC timer with the BSP-inherited `lapic_timer_count`, `sti`, hlt-idle) and `sched_next` hands a non-BSP CPU ONLY its own idle — so the APs wake and idle FOREVER, never running a real proc. **Artifact:** bare production `build/agnos` **1,220,416 B**, flashed via `install-media.sh --update` (ESP-only — agnos-fs `/bin` untouched). **QEMU `-smp 4` pre-flash: CLEAN** — `smp: cpus online: 4`, all APs woke+parked, `Activating scheduler` → `kybernet` → `exec /bin/agnsh` reached, `check.sh` 11/11 · `ring3-smoke` 8/8 · `thread-smoke` PASS on the woken build (QEMU can't model real-Zen INIT-SIPI timing or the iron-only keyboard-death, so this proves only boot-continuity + no-logic-regression — iron is the real test). **WHAT THE FB PHOTO MUST SHOW (read the framebuffer, NEVER CMOS — [[feedback_fb_logs_over_cmos]]):** ① `smp: cpus online: 4` (NOT `AP wake gated`, NOT `cpus online: 1`) = the wake worked + all 3 APs checked in on real Zen; ② boot proceeds past it to `Activating scheduler...` → the agnsh `[ASSIST] >` prompt; ③ **type a multi-command sequence** (`help`↵ `version`↵ `mode`↵) — the keyboard MUST survive (this is the STEP-1 acceptance bar: woken-but-idle APs do NOT keyboard-death, validating the AP LVT LINT0/LINT1 mask `0xFEE00350`/`0x360`). **PASS = `cpus online: 4` + typeable multi-command agnsh prompt on real Zen, no hang/reset.** **FALSIFICATION + next steps:** (a) boot HANGS at the wake / `cpus online` never reaches 4 ⟹ real-Zen INIT-SIPI timing or a per-CPU-state bug — the 1.44.21 bounded ICR delivery-status polls (`ICR_DELIVERY_WAIT`) should fail-safe rather than hang; next = MADT AP-enum, per-Zen SIPI vector/delay tuning, read the FB for a fault bar (= triple-fault) vs a clean stop. (b) `cpus online: 4` BUT keyboard dies after command 1 (the 1.44.x burn-3..6 symptom, now believed `hid_poll` EP-stall-bound, [[project_archaemenid_keystroke_path_usb_only]]) ⟹ the woken AP LVT mask is INSUFFICIENT against the USB-keyboard path — un-flip (`smp_wake_enabled=0`, the documented one-liner) and reassess whether STEP-1 is even compatible with the xHCI keystroke path on this box. **KILL-SWITCH:** `smp_wake_enabled=0` reverts the entire wake (back to the iron-proven 1.45.x single-core boot); STEP-2 (`smp_sched_aps=1` = real procs on APs) is a SEPARATE later burn that rides only AFTER STEP-1 passes. NO auto-run per [[feedback_iron_burns_block_other_work]] — the user reboots archaemenid into the flashed kernel + captures the FB photo. (Reconnect note for the post-burn session: archaemenid IS the dev host, so this Linux session drops the moment it reboots into AGNOS; on reconnect, scan top-level + `iron-nuc-zen-photos/` for the freshest `*.jpg` and read THIS entry's PASS/FALSIFY map first.)

**🔬 SMP SUB-BITE 7 STEP-1 — BURN RAN 2026-06-24 → #GP → ROOT-CAUSED + FIXED + QEMU-VALIDATED BURN-FREE → cut 1.46.1, RE-FLASH + RE-BURN PENDING (2026-06-25).** The STEP-1 burn (woken `build/agnos` 1,220,416 B) **half-passed: `smp: cpus online: 4` on real Zen ✅** (the AP wake works — the H2 riskiest item cleared), boot proceeded through net/kybernet/`exec /bin/agnsh` and agnsh printed its banner — **then a wide BLUE FB bar at the top + halt** (user photo `1460_smp_test.jpg`). The blue bar is the 1.45.16 idt.cyr fault canary (`eax=0x000000FF` = blue in BGRX) = **a CAUGHT CPU exception**, not a hang. CMOS confirmed `0x54=0x0D` (#GP), `0x55=0xE5` (fresh).
- **Root-caused + fixed ENTIRELY BURN-FREE** — a QEMU `-smp 4` + `-cpu max` (TCG, so `-d int` logs guest exceptions — KVM wouldn't) + `qemu-xhci`/`usb-kbd` + `-d int` harness (adapted from `agnsh-multijob-test.py`) reproduced the #GP **3/3 deterministically** and gave the exact errcode + `CS:RIP` for free. **This refutes the standing "SMP is iron-only / read FB never CMOS" framing for SOFTWARE races** — only the LVT keyboard-death + real-Zen LAPIC timing are genuinely iron-only. [[feedback_smp_step1_reproducible_in_qemu]].
- **THREE real STEP-1 bugs (fixed at 1.46.1):** (1) **AP trampoline never reloaded CS** — `jmp far 0x18:..` enters 64-bit via the trampoline GDT (`0x18`=code64), then `lgdt [kernel GDT]` left the cached CS, so `ap_entry` ran with `CS=0x18` = USER DS in the kernel GDT → every AP timer frame pushed `CS=0x18`, `proc_save_context` persisted it into the idle's `proc_cs`, the resume `iretq` #GP'd (errcode `0x18`; iretq frame `CS=0x18`/`RIP=kernel_idle_loop`/`SS=0x10`, GDT pristine). FIX = `lretq` far-return to `CS=0x08` after RSP is set (`smp.cyr`). (2) **AP `sched_next` fell back to proc 0 (kmain)** — exposed by fix 1; an idle-only AP with nothing pickable ran kmain on two CPUs on the BSP's stack → `#PF`/`#GP`/`#UD` cascade at kybernet init. FIX = `if (pcpu_cpu()!=0) return sched_idle_pid_get();` (`sched.cyr`). (3) **boot-order race** — `smp_start_aps` returned on `ap_online_mask` (set early) while APs were registering, then `proc_count=0`/`proc_on_cpu_init` wiped their idle slots. FIX = wake after the proc-table bootstrap + wait on `pcpu_ap_ready` (`main.cyr`/`smp.cyr`).
- **QEMU `-smp 4` validation (post-fix):** interactive agnsh — prompt reached, `sleep2 &` runs to completion while the prompt stays live, repeated `version` answered under preemption, **ZERO faults**. `check.sh` 11/11, `agnsh-smoke`, `ring3-smoke` 5/5, `thread-smoke`, `smp-smoke` (`-smp 4`) all green; all three fixes inert single-core. `build/agnos` 1,221,072 B.
- **NEXT BURN (re-flash via `install-usb.sh --update`, ESP-only):** the #GP is gone in QEMU, so the burn now tests the **genuinely iron-only** unknowns. **PASS = `cpus online: 4` + the agnsh `[ASSIST] >` prompt + a multi-command sequence (`help`↵ `version`↵ `mode`↵) the keyboard SURVIVES on real Zen.** **FALSIFY:** (a) a blue fault-bar again ⟹ a *different* iron-only fault — read CMOS `0x54` for the vector (the #GP capture is still #PF-only; if a new vector appears, add the errcode+RIP capture for it). GDT[3] descriptor is VALID (`0x00CFF2000000FFFF`, `gdt.cyr:17`: P=1,DPL=3,data,writable) and the GDT page is `0x83`-writable in every CR3 (`proc.cyr:425,499`) so the A-bit RMW can't fault. The difference is the iretq's EXECUTION CONTEXT, not the selector or the descriptor.

But **agnos COLLAPSED the KPTI split in 1.40.3** — `proc_create_address_space` stashes the FULL per-proc pml4 (`new_pml4`) as the "user" CR3 in PD[511] (`proc.cyr:486`) and `proc_get_user_cr3` reads it back ⟹ **user CR3 ≡ kernel CR3** for every proc. Both iretqs run under the SAME pml4; the proposed CR3-swap "fix" would `mov cr3` to the value already loaded = INERT. (Verified by reading the AS construction BEFORE writing the user-approved CR3-swap fix — it would have wasted the burn.)

New `idt.cyr` v==13 capture derefs the faulting iretq's OWN saved RSP (`[rsp+0x20]`; long mode always pushes SS:RSP, so this = the timer-ISR iretq frame = `isr_rsp+120`) to stamp the frame the iretq was CONSUMING → virgin low-bank CMOS: **`0x70` loaded SS · `0x71` loaded CS (THE discriminator: `0x23` consistent / `0x08` tear) · `0x72/0x73` loaded RFLAGS · `0x74/0x75` loaded RSP · `0x76/0x77` frame ptr · `0x78/0x79` CR3-at-fault · `0x7A` kernel RFLAGS · `0x7B-0x7E` `do_context_switch` heartbeat (old/new/ctr/magic `0x5C`, `sched.cyr` `g_switch_log`, stamped after `sched_fix_live_frame`, never touches the iretq frame) · `0x7F` GDT[3] access byte read via live `sgdt` (expect `0xF2`)**. `read-boot-log.cyr` decodes all of it. **Verified (mine, not the agent's word):** build pin-honest `cyrius 6.2.36` (same toolchain as the burned `ac210bf`), `build/agnos` **1,235,040 B**; the `sgdt`/GDT[3] hand-asm objdump-disassembles byte-exact (`0f 01 00`=`sgdt [rax]` → `48 8b 40 02`=`mov rax,[rax+2]` → `8a 40 1d`=`mov al,[rax+0x1d]`); `check.sh` **11/11**, `agnsh-smoke` PASS (boots to `[ASSIST]>` in QEMU — the #GP is iron-only, instrument inert). `exc_stub` grown `96→160` u64 (`boot_data.cyr`; #GP stub ~432 B, total ~878 < 1280) + a 16 B `gdtr_scratch` sgdt target.
- **THE DIAGNOSTIC BURN (flash `install-usb.sh --update`, ESP-only; agnos-fs untouched). Expect the same halt + red FB bar after the banner — the CMOS bytes ARE the deliverable.** Read `read-boot-log --verbose`, FIRST-HIT-WINS: **`0x7E`≠`0x5C` (heartbeat absent)** ⟹ the resume iretq is NOT the fault site (`do_context_switch` never reached the heartbeat) ⟹ RE-SCOPE, fault is upstream. **`0x71`=`0x08`** ⟹ tear genuinely revives despite the consistent read ⟹ a writer between `:383` and the iretq ⟹ escalate to a pre-iretq hand-asm stamp in `pic.cyr`. **`0x72/73` RFLAGS≠`0x0202` or `0x75` RSP byte=`0x00`** ⟹ H3 (garbage frame contents) ⟹ fix the saved frame. **`0x7F`≠`0xF2`** ⟹ H2 (GDT[3] corrupted / GDTR relocated) ⟹ targeted GDT-state fix. **`0x71`=`0x23` + RFLAGS=`0x0202` + RSP sane + `0x7F`=`0xF2`** ⟹ the hard truth PROVEN: a genuinely valid frame + valid descriptor rejected by Zen's first inter-privilege timer iretq under the per-proc CR3 ⟹ next fix = the `enter_ring3`-trampoline relocation (rebuild the resume frame at `0x7000`, the one path Zen accepts). ONE burn ⟹ exactly one branch, no theory, no torn-frame re-chase. **IF=0 revert PROHIBITED (user, P0 — SMP depends on IF=1).** No auto-run [[feedback_iron_burns_block_other_work]]; read FB + CMOS [[feedback_fb_logs_over_cmos]] — user reboots + captures (this Linux session drops when archaemenid reboots into AGNOS; on reconnect read THIS entry's read-table first).

**🎯 SMP SUB-BITE 7 — PURE-DIAGNOSTIC BURN DECODED → ROOT-CAUSED to AMD SYSRET-SS-RPL (`STAR[63:48]=0x10`) → torn-frame theory DEAD (decisively, again) → FIXED (one byte) + QEMU-BUILT → CONFIRMING BURN PENDING (2026-06-26, this session).** The staged pure-diagnostic `build/agnos` (`ac210bf`, `exec_preempt=1`/`smp_wake_enabled=1`/`smp_sched_aps=0`, the `idt.cyr` v==13 loaded-frame capture) re-burned; same FB (`smp: cpus online: 4` ✅ → full boot → `agnoshi 1.7.0` banner → blue fault-bar + halt). `read-boot-log --verbose` gave the FULL loaded-frame datum: **`0x54=0x0D` (#GP) · errcode `0x0018` · faulting RIP `0x1be5cd` · fault CS `0x08` · LOADED CS `0x71=0x23` · LOADED SS `0x70=0x18` · LOADED RFLAGS `0x72/73=0x0202` · LOADED RSP byte3 `0x75=0x3f` · CR3-at-fault nonzero · heartbeat `0x7B-0x7E` = old 1 / new 0 / ctr 60 / magic `0x5C` · GDT[3] access `0x7F=0xF3`.**
- **DECODE — the read-table's own "hard-truth" branch, but it MIS-NAMED the gun.** The pre-registered table (entry above) treated `0x71` (CS) as THE discriminator and assumed SS would read `0x1B`. The CS came back `0x23` (consistent ring-3 code, RPL3) — so it is NOT a `CS=0x08` tear; **the torn-frame theory is IRON-FALSIFIED a second time, conclusively.** But the actual smoking gun is **`0x70` (LOADED SS) = `0x18`, NOT `0x1B`** — index 3 (user DS) with **RPL stripped to 0**. `iretq` returning to CPL3 (CS.RPL=3) requires SS.RPL==3; `0x18` has RPL 0 → `#GP`(errcode 0x18 = that SS). And **`0x7F=0xF3` is NOT corruption** — it is `0xF2 | 0x01` = the descriptor's **Accessed bit**, which the CPU sets the instant SS is loaded; GDT[3] (`0x00CFF2000000FFFF`) is a perfectly valid DPL-3 writable-data segment. So the frame is **invalid (SS.RPL=0) and correctly rejected**, NOT "a valid frame rejected by Zen." The table's branch (relocate the `enter_ring3` trampoline) would have been the wrong fix.
- **ROOT CAUSE — `STAR[63:48]=0x10` + AMD SYSRET does NOT force SS.RPL.** `syscall_msr_init` (`syscall_hw.cyr:157`) wrote STAR high = `0x00100008` → SYSRET-base `0x10`. On SYSRET-to-64-bit the CPU computes CS=base+16=0x20 and SS=base+8=0x18. **AMD64 APM Vol 3 (24594) §SYSRET, verbatim:** *"the CPL is forced to 3 … The SS selector is updated to point to the next descriptor-table entry after the CS descriptor (STAR.SYSRET_CS + 8), and **its RPL is NOT forced to 3.**"* So on real Zen, SYSRET forces CS.RPL=3 (→0x23 ✓) but loads SS **verbatim** = `0x18` (RPL 0). After agnsh's FIRST syscall returns, it runs in ring 3 with `SS=0x18`; every later timer tick pushes that, and the next **early-return** tick's ISR-epilogue `iretq` (which bypasses the `sched_fix_live_frame` repair) `#GP`s. **Intel (SDM) AND QEMU TCG both OR 3 into SS** (QEMU `helper_sysret`, post bug #1428352) — that is exactly why QEMU never reproduced it across 245k int-lines and it stayed iron-only for two weeks. Verified this session by a 2-agent workflow (AMD APM/Intel SDM/QEMU-source research @ high confidence + adversarial kernel refutation: **NO other `SS=0x18` source exists** — every literal ring-3 SS in the tree is `0x1B`; the single `48 0F 07` SYSRETQ is the unique seed, which `proc_save_context` then propagates into `proc_ss[]`).
- **WHY IT SURVIVED ~60 switches (the early-return-tick guard-bypass).** `do_context_switch` early-returns at `sched_active==0` / `preempt_count>0` / `proc_count<2` / `new==old` — **all upstream of `sched_fix_live_frame` (`sched.cyr:393`)**. On a FULL switch the guard sees `SS=0x18 != want 0x1B`, repairs it to `0x1B`, and that iretq succeeds — so full switches are immune. The fault needs the window where agnsh is running post-SYSRET (`SS=0x18`) AND a tick early-returns (`new==old==agnsh` on the BSP — common at the idle prompt once the AP-claimed idles are `on_cpu`-skipped by `sched_next`). The heartbeat names the last FULL switch (old 1 / new 0, 60 in); the faulting iretq is a later early-return tick that writes no heartbeat and runs no guard — which is precisely why **no `0x65=0x3C` stamp ever appeared** (the guard was never on this code path; it was misdirected the whole time, NOT a substitute for the SYSRET fix).
- **FIX (one byte, Linux-canonical) — `syscall_hw.cyr:157` `0xBA;0x08;0x00;0x10;0x00` → `…0x13…`** (STAR[63:48] `0x10`→`0x13` = `0x10 | 3`, RPL-3 baked into the base). SYSRET then computes CS=0x13+16=0x23 and SS=0x13+8=0x1B with RPL 3 *carried by the arithmetic* (the +8/+16 don't disturb the low 2 bits) — no reliance on the CPU to force it. SYSCALL entry (STAR[47:32]=0x08, unchanged) and `base+0` (compat 32-bit SYSRET, never emitted) are unaffected; descriptors at base+8 (GDT[3] userDS) / base+16 (GDT[4] userCS) are valid. This is exactly Linux's `MSR_STAR=__USER32_CS<<48` trick (`__USER32_CS=0x23`, RPL baked). Comments at `:143`/`:556` corrected (they falsely claimed "SS=base+8|3"). **Built clean: `build/agnos` 1,235,040 B — byte-size-IDENTICAL to the diagnostic build** (an immediate-byte change, no codegen shift); multiboot2 OK, entry `0x1000a8`; pin-honest cyrius `6.2.36`.
- **THE CONFIRMING BURN (flash `install-usb.sh --update`, ESP-only; agnos-fs untouched).** The instrumentation is KEPT (it now becomes the proof). **PASS** = NO blue fault-bar; boot proceeds PAST the `agnoshi 1.7.0` banner to `[ASSIST] >`; a multi-command sequence (`help`↵ `version`↵ `mode`↵) survives on real Zen — **the 2-week IF=1 blocker DEAD, the 1.46.x boot-to-shell milestone**. **CMOS `read-boot-log --verbose`:** `0x54 != 0x0D` (no #GP) is the clean pass; if anything still stamps, the gold datum is again **`0x70` (LOADED SS) — it MUST now read `0x1B`** (proves SYSRET yields the RPL-3 SS). **FALSIFY:** (i) still a blue bar + `0x54=0x0D` + `0x70=0x18` ⟹ the STAR write didn't take effect on this silicon (re-read STAR via a diagnostic, or a SECOND SYSRET path / MSR re-write resets it — but `syscall_init` is the only STAR writer); (ii) still #GP but `0x70=0x1B` + `0x71=0x23` ⟹ a genuinely-different residual (now we really are at "valid frame" territory — escalate to the `enter_ring3`-trampoline-relocation lead the old table named); (iii) `cpus online: 4` then keyboard dies after cmd 1 ⟹ AP LVT mask vs the xHCI keystroke path ([[project_archaemenid_keystroke_path_usb_only]]), orthogonal. **STEP-2 (`smp_sched_aps=1`) very likely rides on the SAME fix** — AP-resumed ring-3 procs return via the same SYSRET, so the SS=0x18 seed was almost certainly STEP-2's blocker too; re-evaluate after STEP-1-IF=1 is iron-green. `exec_preempt=1` kept (P0: SMP depends on IF=1; IF=0 revert PROHIBITED). **Arc.** The first network-consuming-app cycle. Every kernel-side hook `tls_native` binds to landed + was hardened: `getrandom`#45 / `time_unix`#46 (Zen-RDRAND entropy + RTC cert clock), client TCP `sock_connect`#47–`sock_close`#50, UDP-53 `udp_bind`#51–`udp_unbind`#54, `icmp_echo`#55, server `sock_listen`#56 / `sock_accept`#57, and the file group `lseek`#58 / `flock`#59 / `winsize`#60 (1.45.13). A 6-lens adversarial sweep (1.45.6) fixed 4 CRITICALs. **1.45.14** is the cyrius-pin-honest cut (6.2.30→6.2.36, kernel `cmp`-byte-identical). All QEMU/Docker/SLIRP-validated — but the **whole 1.45.x frontier is iron-UNvalidated** (last iron evidence = **1.44.23 burn-5, 2026-06-13** — boot-to-shell + agnsh launches, but keyboard died after command 1).

**What the production 1.45.14 burn artifact carries to iron, none of it iron-tested:**
1. **The BURN-6 xHCI keyboard-ring fix (1.44.25)** — `hid_kbd_configure` arms the kbd interrupt-IN ring **16-deep** (was 1) + `hid_poll` tops up + a read-entry `hid_kbd_kick()` doorbell restart (`usb/hid.cyr:379` + `core/syscall.cyr:299`). The cure for the burn-3/5 "keyboard dies after command 1" EP-stall ([[project_archaemenid_keystroke_path_usb_only]]). ⚠ **state.md asserts this "held (2026-06-14)" but there is NO BURN-6 iron photo or iron-log entry** — newest photo is burn-3 (Jun 13). The close-out burn is the first clean test.
2. **The SMP single-core gate** (`smp_wake_enabled=0`) — boot stays single-core cooperative (MVP-proven; SMP wake parked).
3. **The entire 1.45.x ring-3 net + `winsize` surface**, exercised by the staged userland tools `iam` / `kii` / `cyim` / `dig` / `yo` / `whirl` / `kriya`+coreutils / `owl`-cat (**sans chakshu** — Linux-signalfd TUI, no agnos build). None has run on real Zen; last iron userland exec was DOOM (1.43.8).

**HYPOTHESIS — dependency-ordered; each gates the next:**

- **Diagnosis (13-agent workflow + 3-lens adversarial refute, all source-cited):** the tools resolve via **off-subnet** public fallbacks (`yo` → 1.1.1.1 `src/dns.cyr:37`; `taar`/whirl → 8.8.8.8) because `/etc/resolv.conf` is absent on agnos **and no syscall exposed the kernel's DHCP-leased on-subnet resolver** (`net_dhcp.cyr:283` `net_dns_server`). Off-subnet DNS needs working gateway→internet routing the QEMU `dig` smoke never exercised (it used an explicit on-subnet `@10.0.2.3`; SLIRP also forwards broadcast-framed/off-subnet traffic that a real switch drops). **Refuted (3/3 each):** clock-frozen-timeout (the recv loops interleave `sleep_ms`#41 + bound on `uptime_ms`#40 → they self-terminate) and dead-LAPIC-timer (**DOOM@1439 runs IF=0 paced by the exact `sti`+`hlt` window**; these sti-windows are CPL0→CPL0, not the unvalidated CPL3→CPL0 path). So a *clean* no-reply is a ~5–20 s timeout, not a permanent freeze — a true hard-lock implies a kernel FAULT past DNS (hence the canary).

**1.45.16 FIX + CANARY — `net_config`#61 (on-subnet resolver) + net-stage FB beacon + fault-bar. BURN RAN 2026-06-23 — net_config#61 IRON-CONFIRMED; surfaced the RX-drain net bug fixed at 1.45.17 (result below).**
- **The fix:** new syscall **`net_config(field)`#61** (`kernel/core/syscall.cyr`) returns the kernel net config to ring 3 (field 3 = `net_dns_server`, the DHCP option-6 on-subnet resolver). `taar` (`_taar_plat_dns_server`→`_taar_resolv_discover`), `yo` (`platform_dns_server`→`_dns_choose_nameserver`) **and `dig`** (`resolv_discover` — bare `dig <host>` only; the `@server` path was always unaffected) now prefer it over the off-subnet fallback. Cut as releases: **taar 0.3.1 · yo 0.5.7 · whirl 0.6.2 · dig 0.3.5** (whirl/yo/dig pin taar 0.3.1 → **tag taar FIRST**). archaemenid is `192.168.1.0/24` gw `.1` (matches the agnos static fallback), so the leased resolver is on-subnet + directly ARP-reachable — the QEMU on-subnet path that worked.
- **The canary** (diagnostic, FB-over-CMOS — distinguishes WHERE + spin-vs-fault on one burn):
  - **Top-RIGHT corner square** (`fb_dbg_beacon`) = the last net stage reached: 🟩 green = resolver fetched (`net_config`#61) · 🟨 yellow = DNS/UDP tx · 🟧 orange = TCP connect · 🟦 cyan = TCP send (TLS/HTTP) · 🟪 magenta = ICMP ping.
  - **Wide bar across the TOP** = a CPU fault was CAUGHT (`idt.cyr` `rep stosd` after the CMOS vector stamp). Bar present ⟹ **FAULT** (read CMOS `0x54`/`0x55`/CR2 via `read-boot-log` for the exact vector); bar absent + frozen ⟹ **SPIN** at the stage the corner color names.
- **Deploy (BOTH needed for the fix):** (1) kernel via `install-usb.sh --update` (ESP) → `net_config`#61 + canary; (2) **mount-copy the freshly-staged net tools `build/rootfs/bin/{yo,whirl,dig}` onto the agnos-fs `/bin`** (yo 0.5.7 · whirl 0.6.2 · dig 0.3.5 — the old staged tools don't call `net_config`) — `--update` is ESP-only, so without the mount-copy the burn shows the canary but still uses the off-subnet fallback. **All tools re-staged fresh** (`stage-tools.sh --build`, 2026-06-23) — the net trio carries the fix; the H1/H2 re-confirm set (`iam`/`bnrmr`/`anuenue`/`kii`/`cyim`/`kriya`+coreutils) is current too. Only **`owl` did not rebuild** (1.4.2 agnos blocker = `sit`'s `syscall(SYS_CHDIR)`, queued as a 1.46.x item) — its stale cat-only build stays staged, harmless for this burn. Kernel-only flash still gives canary diagnostics with the old tools.
