**🎯 1.45.16 BURN RESULT (2026-06-23, photos `iron-nuc-zen-photos/14516-agnos-1.45.16-*`) — net_config#61 ✅ WORKS on iron; a REAL kernel net bug found + FIXED at 1.45.17 (burn-free).**
- **✅ Boot + net_config:** fully clean boot across all 6 frames (ext2 multi-chunk holds, DHCP `ACK ip=192.168.1.182 gw=192.168.1.1 dns=192.168.1.1`, ARP gateway cached, `net: L2 OK`, agnsh 1.7.0). **`yo google.com` RESOLVES on-subnet → `142.251.32.174`** — the 1.45.16 net_config#61 on-subnet-resolver fix is **IRON-CONFIRMED**, and the 1.45.15 DNS-prefix lockup is GONE (clean errors, prompt returns each time, **no fault-bar in any photo** → no kernel fault; the canary worked — magenta=ping on `yo`, yellow=DNS-tx on `whirl`/`dig`).
- **❌ NEW real net bug:** `yo` pings **2/4 = 50% loss**; `dig google.com` → "no servers could be reached"; `whirl https://google.com` → "connection failed". Systematically ruled out: **NOT environmental** (user's LAN is fast; `yo`=0% loss on Linux; `dig` couldn't get a reply from `192.168.1.1` — the LOCAL router one ARP-hop away — which is internet-irrelevant); **NOT a stale deploy** (all three on-disk `/bin` tools verified **byte-identical to the fresh net_config builds** via a read-only `nvme0n1p2` agnos-fs mount + `cmp`); **NOT a tool code bug** (dig's & yo's net_config+UDP paths are equivalent, and dig retries MORE — 3×5 s vs 2×2 s; the failure is `QRY_TIMEOUT` = bound+sent, no reply).
- **ROOT CAUSE (source-traced):** `timer_handler` (`agnos/kernel/arch/x86_64/pic.cyr`) bumps the tick + context-switches but **never serviced the NIC** — the RX ring was drained ONLY inside a tool's own `net_poll()` loop. During `sleep_ms` ping pacing / between commands / idle the r8169 ring went unserviced, so on a live LAN broadcast+multicast chatter overran the 64-deep ring (`missed`=FIFO-overflow, the documented 1.32.7 signature) and the NIC dropped frames — including the tools' replies. **Invisible in QEMU because SLIRP is point-to-point + chatter-free** (the ring never fills) — the inverse of the usual "QEMU catches what Docker can't" ([[feedback_qemu_test_agnos_userland]]): here QEMU *hid* an iron bug, because net-**loss** needs real-LAN chatter SLIRP can't manufacture.
- **REPRODUCED THE INVERSE BURN-FREE:** production kernel + the real `--agnos` tools driven through agnsh via xHCI `usb-kbd` over SLIRP virtio-net (`scratchpad/net_qemu_confirm.py`) gave **`yo` 4/4 · 0% loss** (gateway AND via net_config) + **`dig` explicit + bare both NOERROR** — proving the net-stack LOGIC sound and isolating the drop to the r8169 RX poll cadence. (Aside: QEMU RTT shows 10/20 ms vs Linux 5 ms = the separate `arch_wait`=`hlt`-per-10 ms-tick quantization — cosmetic, deferred.)
- **FIX — agnos 1.45.17 (`pic.cyr` + `kernel/core/net_ingress.cyr`):** `timer_handler` now calls a new `net_rx_drain()` every 100 Hz tick so the ring stays drained regardless of userland activity. `net_poll()` keeps its prior contract (TX retransmits + RX drain) for all tool/recv-loop callers; `net_rx_drain()` is the RX-only inner loop (no TX from interrupt context). A `net_poll_busy` re-entrancy guard makes a tick landing mid-drain (tool loops run IF=1 in the `sleep_ms` sti-window) a no-op rather than corrupt the shared `net_rx_pkt`/`r8169_rx_idx`; drains before `do_context_switch`, `nic_ready()`-gated (early-boot ticks safe). **Validated:** QEMU regression-clean — boot-to-shell, `yo`/`dig` 0% loss, both DNS paths NOERROR, `check.sh` 11/11, `agnsh-smoke` PASS; `build/agnos` **1,209,880 B**.
- **DEPLOY + 1.45.17 BURN EXPECTATIONS:** **kernel-only — rides `install-usb.sh --update` (ESP), NO agnos-fs mount-copy** (staged tools untouched). Re-run `yo google.com` (×4) + `dig google.com` + `whirl https://google.com`: ✅ **PASS** ⟹ `yo` **4/4 · 0% loss**, `dig` resolves (NOERROR + prompt), `whirl` connects (HTTPS result or a clean HTTP-level error) — the 50%→0% is iron-confirmed and the net cycle's H3 closes; ❌ **FALSIFIES** the chatter-overrun root cause ⟹ still drops with the ISR draining — next: r8169 RX-ring-pointer desync (`r8169_rx_idx` vs the chip write pointer) or a deeper `r8169_poll` defect. Capture an FB photo, capture it into `iron-nuc-zen-photos/` as `14517-agnos-1.45.17-<symptom>.jpg`. NO auto-run per [[feedback_iron_burns_block_other_work]].

**🎯 1.45.17 CLOSE-OUT BURN RESULT (2026-06-23, no FB photo captured — user verbal report) — net tools work at DECENT CAPACITY on iron; H3 net closes; 1.45.x CYCLE CLOSED.**
- **✅ Net path functional on real Zen.** The 1.45.17 `net_rx_drain()`-from-`timer_handler` fix landed: the ~50% RX packet loss of the 1.45.16 burn is gone, and the net tools (`yo` / `dig` / `whirl`) resolve + connect at **decent capacity** (the user's report — **not** a claim of a perfect 4/4 · 0% loss, and no FB photo this burn). The chatter-overrun root cause holds: draining the r8169 RX ring every 100 Hz tick keeps the ring from overrunning during idle/`sleep_ms` gaps on a live LAN.
- **All three pre-registered hypotheses PASS** — **H1 keyboard ✅** (1.45.14 burn: the burn-6 xHCI 16-deep kbd-ring + `hid_kbd_kick` fix from 1.44.25 holds on real Zen, multi-command input survives) · **H2 userland-exec ✅** (1.45.14/15 burns: tools exec from the ext2 agnos-fs after the multi-chunk-BGDT fix) · **H3 net ✅** (this burn, decent capacity) ⟹ **1.45.x is iron-validated end-to-end (boot · keyboard · userland · net) and the cycle CLOSES.**
- **Carry-forward (deferred — NOT a 1.45.x blocker):** thorough per-project testing of each net tool (`yo`/`dig`/`whirl`/`taar` — per-tool packet loss/latency, edge cases, error paths) when more time can be dedicated; the `anuenue` rainbow-coloring ≠ Linux polish (anuenue backlog); `owl`→`sit`/`SYS_CHDIR` agnos port (1.46.x). The `arch_wait`=`hlt`-per-tick RTT quantization stays cosmetic/deferred. (**The `kriya ls` topline was RESOLVED** — it was the same ext2 multi-chunk-BGDT bug, cleared by the 1.45.15 fix, not a kriya bug.)
- **No new kernel work owed by the close-out** — 1.45.17 is the final cut of the cycle. VERSION stays **1.45.17** pending the user's close tag ([[feedback_no_unprompted_version_bumps]]).

**🎯 IRON BURN 1 (2026-06-12) — FAIL → ROOT-CAUSED + FIXED: BSP TSS.RSP0 sat in live kernel .bss; the first ring-3 IF=1 timer tick corrupted kernel globals → triple-fault lockup at the agnoshi banner.**

**Burn result (user report):** boot reached the `agnoshi 1.7.0` banner line, then **locked up** — no shell header ("AI-native shell…" / "Built-ins:…"), no `[ASSIST] >` prompt. A freeze, not a reset. **Positive finding:** reaching the agnsh banner means the whole pre-scheduler boot path — including the **SMP-AP wake (1.44.18), the flagged riskiest item** — PASSED on real Zen (kybernet only launches agnsh *after* SMP wake). The failure is the NEXT new-on-iron thing: agnsh is the first ring-3 proc to run preemptible (IF=1, 1.44.14), so the first 100 Hz timer tick takes the **first-ever cross-privilege (CPL3→CPL0) interrupt that does real work** on hardware.

**Root cause (triple-verified — a 6-reader + adversarial-3-lens diagnostic workflow, independent ELF re-derivation, and the kernel's own source all converge).** The BSP kernel interrupt stack `TSS.RSP0 = 0x200000` (`agnos/kernel/arch/x86_64/gdt.cyr`) sits **inside the live kernel image**: the ELF LOAD segment is `VirtAddr 0x100000 / MemSiz 0x1334E8` → image+BSS spans `[0x100000..0x2334E8)`, so `0x200000` is ~210 KB *inside* live `.bss`. On the first CPL3→CPL0 timer tick the CPU loads RSP=RSP0 and pushes the 5-word frame; the ISR (`pic.cyr`) pushes 15 GPRs; `timer_handler`→`do_context_switch` then runs its whole call tree on that stack — all **growing DOWN into live kernel globals**. `do_context_switch` immediately reads the now-garbage `sched_active`/`preempt_count`/`proc_count` → corrupt switch / corrupt iretq frame → a #GP/#PF delivered via the *same* poisoned RSP0 → #DF → triple-fault → silent freeze, exactly between the banner and the header.

**Why it hid until now.** Every prior iron win ran ring-3 **IF=0** (foreground `execwait`#37 / DOOM — run-to-completion, never preempted), so the CPL3→CPL0 path that consults RSP0 to *do work* never ran. The only prior ring-3 fault (the 1.40.9 #PF) just `cli;hlt`'d — it never grew a call tree into globals. The SYSCALL path is immune (it uses `syscall_kstack_top = 0x3F0000`, bypassing the TSS), which is why every `write()`/`exit()` — including the banner print itself — always worked. QEMU TCG counts ticks in *retired instructions*, so agnsh reaches its blocking `read()` before the first CPL3 tick → **the bug is invisible in QEMU** (`agnsh-smoke` prints the full banner + prompt). The kernel had already relocated its *other* region-1 stacks off `0x200000` for this exact reason (`pmm.cyr:80` "0x200000-0x210000 are live .bss"; `boot_shim.cyr:25` boot 32-bit stack → 0x380000; syscall kstack → 0x3F0000/0x3D0000) — the BSP TSS.RSP0 was simply the one stack left behind.

**Fix (behavioral, no instrumentation) — cut as agnos 1.44.23 (2026-06-12) in `agnos/kernel/arch/x86_64/gdt.cyr`, BSP RSP0 `0x200000 → 0x3C0000`** (both `tss_kernel_stack` and the `tss_get_cpu_stack(0)` return). `0x3C0000` is above the image end (`0x2334E8`), below the syscall kstacks (`0x3D0000`/`0x3F0000`), clear of the AP RSP0 stacks (`0x320000-0x340000`), inside PD[1] (mirrored identity-supervisor-writable into every per-proc CR3), and inside the PMM-reserved region 1 — the same placement pattern the syscall kstack already uses. Verified SAFE for the IF=0 foreground exec path (never takes a CPL3 interrupt → RSP0 unused → byte-for-byte behavior) and single-core (only the BSP value changes; parked-AP RSP0s untouched). **Two alternatives were adversarially REFUTED and NOT applied:** masking the legacy PIC (`out 0x21,0xFF`) — would KILL keyboard IRQ1 (the iron-proven input path) and there is no IOAPIC to re-route it; and a scheduler "kmain rip=0" guard — its premise is false (proc 0 holds a real saved context by agnsh-launch time, via the kmain↔idle ping-pong).

**Regression gate (QEMU — cannot reproduce the iron timing, so this is a no-regression check only):** production `build/agnos` **1,193,424 B** clean; `ring3-smoke` **8/8** (ring-3 preemption — the exact path); `smp-smoke` **3/3** (TSS init, all 4 CPUs); `agnsh-smoke` PASS (full banner + prompt); `check.sh` **11/11**; `sweep.sh` **7/7**.

**Re-burn falsification criteria.**
- ✅ **PASS:** boot prints the `agnoshi 1.7.0` banner AND the "AI-native shell…" / "Built-ins:…" header AND a live `[ASSIST] >` prompt (the timer now preempts agnsh across the relocated RSP0 stack without corrupting globals). This also re-confirms SMP wake + the whole 1.44.x preemptive stack on iron.
- ❌ **FALSIFIES (still locks at/after the banner):** the RSP0 relocation is not the (whole) cause — next suspect is the LAPIC EOI under agnsh's per-process CR3 (`apic_eoi` writes `0xFEE000B0` *after* `do_context_switch`; re-verify the APIC is in the per-proc PDPT mirror) or a real-Zen LAPIC + IF=1 interaction. The revert is one constant, but unnecessary — the change cannot regress the IF=0 paths.
- ⚠️ If the banner + prompt show but a `&` bg-job input line truncates: unrelated (the 1.44.21 nbread clamp; byte-identical for the foreground prompt — not expected).

NO auto-run per [[feedback_iron_burns_block_other_work]] — the user flashes + burns.

**🎯 IRON BURN 2 (2026-06-12, agnos 1.44.23) — RSP0 FIX HELD, FAILURE MOVED EARLIER. Two symptoms, both NARROWED via a 44-agent diagnostic workflow (5 readers + 12 hypotheses + adversarial 3-lens verify; verify phase truncated by a session-token limit, key verdicts re-derived against source). Neither is yet fingerprinted — but the kernel ALREADY stamps the answer to CMOS. The gating next step is a FREE read-boot-log, NOT another burn.**

**Burn result (user report + the two photos, now catalogued into `iron-nuc-zen-photos/`):**
- **Symptom A — cold (first) boot** (photo `iron-nuc-zen-photos/14423-agnos-1.44.23-burn2-coldboot-freeze-at-exec-agnsh.jpg`)**:** full green boot through GPT/VFS/ext2(seq=82)/FAT, SYSCALL/SYSRET, stack canary, interrupts, `Timer ticks before sched: 7`, **`smp: cpus online: 4`** (so SMP-AP wake PASSED again on real Zen), `Activating scheduler...`, full DHCP DORA (lease .171), ARP reply, `net: L2 OK`, `Launching kybernet...`, `kybernet: 2 processes`, `kybernet: 31622 free pages`, **`kybernet: exec /bin/agnsh` — then frozen, zero ring-3 output.** At burn 1 (.22) the agnsh banner PRINTED then froze; now the freeze is EARLIER (the user's "regressed more").
- **Symptom B — every warm reboot after** (photo `iron-nuc-zen-photos/14423-agnos-1.44.23-burn2-warmreboot-stuck-dhcp-discover.jpg`)**:** green to `Activating scheduler...` → **`dhcp: DISCOVER`** and apparently never an OFFER. Cold boot DHCP worked perfectly; warm boots don't.

**The RSP0 relocation itself is SOUND (re-derived from source, not assumed).** At 1.44.23 the first CPL3→CPL0 timer tick is a **verified no-op**: agnsh is the only ready non-idle proc, so `do_context_switch` returns at `sched.cyr:171` before saving/restoring anything (agnsh never even transitions to state-2). `0x3C0000` is in PD[1] (identity-supervisor-mapped into every per-proc CR3), ~500 KB clear of AP3's stack top `0x340000`. So the .23 freeze is **not** the first-tick path the .22 fix targeted — the relocation cured what it was meant to cure, and unmasked the next thing.

**Symptom B — UNDERSTOOD (CONFIRMED, incl. a live QEMU repro of the production kernel).** Two layers:
1. **No OFFER on warm boot = r8169 RX-wedge.** The crashed cold-boot session leaves the NIC fully ARMED (CR.RE/TE set, 64-desc RX ring DMA live). The user warm-resets (no power cycle); RTL8168-class MACs preserve DMA/PHY state across a warm reset. Next boot, `r8169_reset` (`r8169.cyr:322-329`) issues a **bare `CR.RST` + poll with no pre-reset quiesce** — unlike Linux's `rtl_rx_close` + RXDV-gate-before-reset on 8168G+. The repo's OWN audit already flagged this (`docs/development/prior-art/r8169-iron-burn-audit.md:74`; the `r8169.cyr:597-617` RXDV_GATED comment cites U-Boot's "DHCP failures after kernel reboots"). **This is NOT a regression:** the ONLY pure AGNOS→AGNOS warm reboot ever logged (1373 boot-2/3, `iron-nuc-zen-log.md:606`) ALSO flaked DHCP; every "multi-boot worked" session interleaved a Linux flash/e2fsck that re-init'd the NIC. Warm-boot DHCP was simply never cleanly exercised before.
2. **The "hangs forever" SHAPE is an observation artifact, not an execution death.** The OFFER wait is a **bounded 800-iteration hlt-paced loop** (`net_dhcp.cyr:153-201`) that prints `dhcp: OFFER timeout`, falls back to static IP (`main.cyr:2955-2957`), and **continues the boot** (boot does NOT gate on net). A verifier booted the actual 1.44.23 `build/agnos` under QEMU `-smp 4` with a dead netdev: `DISCOVER → 16.0 s → OFFER timeout → static fallback → ARP fail → kybernet → agnsh`. On iron the timeout is ~16–21 s (the 1.32.x-era ~8 s, doubled by the new kmain↔idle 2-ticks-per-iteration rotation; LAPIC is human-scale, the "uncalibrated → minutes" idea was refuted by 1.32.x history). **So the warm boot is alive in a ~16–21 s countdown — the user reset before it elapsed.** If left to run it would fall through to static IP and hit Symptom A again at exec.

**Symptom A — NARROWED, not fingerprinted. The two leading mechanisms were adversarially REFUTED; the kernel already holds the discriminator.** The deterministic-looking pre-banner freeze is one of: a caught CPU exception somewhere in the newly-reached ring-3 startup, OR a non-faulting hang in the exec/load pipeline now running under the armed scheduler. The seductive **ring-0-selector SMEP race** (the `init.cyr` launch window between `elf_load_from_file` creating agnsh's slot READY with default ring-0 CS=0x08 and `proc_current = pid`, with no `proc_set_ring3`) is a REAL, QEMU-proven-lethal landmine — but it was **REFUTED as the cause** on two independent grounds: (1) its failure shape is a **triple-fault RESET** (SMEP #PF → SMAP-blocked frame push on the U=1 user stack → #DF → #DF, every IDT gate IST=0), not the observed hang-with-log-on-screen; (2) it is **RSP0/delta-independent** (CPL0 throughout) so it cannot explain a regression that tracks the .22→.23 delta, and the same window was traversed cleanly on ≥6 prior iron boots. It stays a justified one-line hardening, NOT the diagnosis.

**THE GATING NEXT STEP — FREE, no burn, no instrumentation-add, no reflash (the burned binary already carries it):** the kernel stamps every caught exception to CMOS (`idt.cyr`): `0x55=0xE5` magic, `0x54=`vector (`0x0D` #GP / `0x0E` #PF / `0x06` #UD / `0x08` #DF), and for #PF the full 48-bit CR2 (`0x5C-0x62`) + faulting RIP low-32 (`0x63-0x66`); plus a boot-progress byte `CMOS[0x50]` (`0x20`=entered ring 3 via `ring3.cyr:145`, `0x15`=kybernet launch, `0x10`=scheduler armed). **Do ONE power-cycle cold boot** (power cycle, not reset — also clears the NIC wedge so DORA works again, re-confirming Symptom B), let it freeze at exec, then from Linux on archaemenid run `sudo ./scripts/read-boot-log.sh`. Decode:

| `0x50` | `0x55` | `0x54` / CR2 | Verdict |
|--------|--------|--------------|---------|
| `0x20` | `0xE5` | `0x0E`, CR2≈`0x400078` | entered ring 3, #PF fetching agnsh's first instruction (mapping/perms or the SMEP-race cousin) |
| `0x20` | `0xE5` | `0x0E`, CR2≥4 GB or stack VA | ring-3 #PF on data/stack — different fix |
| `0x15` | `0xE5` | any vector | died IN the exec/elf-load pipeline before ring 3 (a caught fault under the armed scheduler) |
| `0x20` or `0x15` | **≠`0xE5`** | — | **non-faulting hang** (spin / IF=0 wait / livelock), not an exception — points at first-CPL3-tick territory or a load-path deadlock |
| `0x10` | ≠`0xE5` | — | (stale from the last warm boot — re-read after a clean cold-only boot) |

This partitions the entire Symptom-A space in one read. (Note `0x50` is last-write-wins, so a fresh cold-only boot gives the cleanest `0x50`; `0x54/0x55` survive warm boots since those don't except.)

**Two prior-art-converged fixes are staged-ready to ride the SAME next burn, regardless of the CMOS read (both independently justified):**
1. **r8169 pre-reset quiesce** (Symptom B root) — port Linux's `rtl_rx_close` + RXDV-gate + ~2 ms settle BEFORE `CR.RST` into `r8169_reset` (`r8169.cyr:322-329`), so a warm boot recovers DHCP without a power cycle.
2. **`proc_set_ring3(pid)` in the kybernet launch window** (latent landmine) — add it in `init.cyr` before `proc_current = pid`, mirroring the #43 path's compensation at `syscall.cyr:1457-1462`; closes the ~1e-5/boot SMEP-race triple-fault.

Neither is bundled with a *Symptom-A behavioral repair* — that waits for the CMOS fingerprint, per [[feedback_iron_burns_block_other_work]] / [[feedback_known_knowledge_first]] (the instrument already exists; read it before writing more).

**Re-burn / read falsification.**
- ✅ **Symptom B confirmed** if the CMOS read shows the cold boot reached `0x20`/`0x15` (CPU alive past net), or a power-cycle cold boot gets a clean DORA while a warm reset does not.
- The CMOS read **fingerprints Symptom A**; only then is the precise behavioral fix written. If `0x55≠0xE5` everywhere (non-faulting hang), the next suspect is the exec/elf-load pipeline under preemption (a disk-poll path suspended by a context switch), NOT an exception.

NO auto-run per [[feedback_iron_burns_block_other_work]] — the user power-cycles, boots once, and reads the log.

**🎯 IRON BURN 3 (2026-06-13, agnos 1.44.23, photo `iron-nuc-zen-photos/14423-agnos-1.44.23-burn3-agnsh-help-renders-freeze-after-first-cmd.jpg`) — THE BIGGEST ADVANCE OF THE 1.44.x ARC ON IRON, plus a clean iron-only root-cause. Burns 1+2 are both CLEARED: agnsh launches FULLY on real Zen.**

**Burn result (user report + the photo):** full green boot — `Timer ticks before sched: 7`, **`smp: cpus online: 4`** (SMP-AP wake confirmed AGAIN), DHCP DORA (lease .172), ARP reply, `net: L2 OK`, `Launching kybernet...`, `kybernet: exec /bin/agnsh`, **`agnoshi 1.7.0` banner + the full "AI-native shell…" / "Built-ins:…" header + a live `[ASSIST] >` prompt**, and **`help` typed + rendered in full**. This is **strictly further than any prior 1.44.x iron boot**.

**WHAT actually got us to the prompt (corrected — NOT the RSP0 fix):** commit `7495fe6` ("repair to kernel - attempt to return to shell", user-authored, the most-recent agnos commit) **DISABLED preemptive agnsh** — `init.cyr` now leaves `exec_preempt = 0`, so `ring3.cyr:136` never sets IF=1 and agnsh launches **IF=0, cooperative, foreground run-to-completion** (the proven 14115/DOOM model). The IF=1 preemptive agnsh (1.44.14) is what froze archaemenid at the banner (Burns 1-2); reverting to IF=0 is what restored boot-to-shell. So the **preemptive context-switch path is OFF on this burn — by construction it cannot be the cause of any Burn-3 symptom.** (The .23 RSP0 relocation rides along but was not the unblocker; an earlier log revision here mis-credited it.)

**New failure (user report):** after a program/builtin runs, **keyboard input dies** —
- `help` (a pure builtin, no exec) prints, then **keyboard is dead** (prompt is live on screen, no input accepted). *"this worked in previous attempts, not 1.44x."*
- `ls` "fails" but a subsequent command still works (keyboard alive at that instant).
- `doom` freezes on the title card (can't advance).

**ROOT CAUSE — the PIT/LAPIC dual-vector-INT32 EOI wedge (fingerprinted from source, not assumed; iron-only by construction).** `pic_init` (`arch/x86_64/pic.cyr`) programs the legacy **PIT channel 0 as a free-running 100 Hz mode-2 rate generator AND leaves IRQ0 unmasked** (master mask `0xFC` = IRQ0+IRQ1 on). `apic_timer_init(32, …)` (`core/main.cyr:87`) then starts the **LAPIC timer on the SAME vector INT32** (the code comment even notes *"same as PIT"*). `timer_handler` (`pic.cyr:34-43`) **always EOIs the LAPIC** (`apic_eoi`, since `apic_enabled==1`) — it never PIC-EOIs. So every time the **PIT** delivers its IRQ0 through the 8259, the PIC's **IRQ0 in-service bit is set and never cleared** → the 8259 (fully-nested mode) then **refuses to deliver any equal-or-lower-priority IRQ**, and **IRQ1 (keyboard) is lower priority than IRQ0** → keyboard interrupts are blocked at the PIC. The **LAPIC timer keeps firing INT32 independently** (its EOI is correct), so the prompt stays alive — *keyboard dies, everything else lives*, exactly the reported shape. **Iron-only:** on archaemenid the keystroke path is `kb_isr`/IRQ1/PIC (`keyboard.cyr:60-68` says so explicitly — *"the producer that delivers on archaemenid… requires IF=1"*); QEMU types via the USB `hid_poll` event ring, which never traverses the PIC → **the wedge is invisible in every QEMU smoke** (agnsh-smoke types a full line + prompt).

**DISPOSITIVE PROOF it's the wedge and NOT any context-switch/resume mechanism (a 17-agent adversarial workflow + independent source re-derivation):** `help` is a **pure builtin** — no exec, no ring-3 child, no `kernel_resume`, no `do_context_switch` — yet the keyboard dies after it *exactly* as after `ls`/`doom`. The cause must therefore be **global, exec-independent, and latched** (works once, then dead). That is precisely the 8259 IRQ0 in-service bit — never a per-proc-CR3 EOI #PF, a shared-kstack clobber, or a GPR/iretq save-restore (all of which require a context switch that a builtin never triggers, and all of which are off the executed path anyway since `exec_preempt=0`).

**On the "why now" (honest residual):** `apic_timer_init` (the LAPIC timer on vector 32) has existed since **2026-04-06** — two months *before* burn 14115 (2026-06-06) where the keyboard worked — so the static PIC/APIC config is **not** what's new. The wedge is a **dwell-time effect**: it bites once the system sits in a sustained IF=1 keyboard read-spin (between/after commands) long enough for a PIT IRQ0 (100 Hz) to land and stick the PIC's IRQ0 ISR. 14115 was a brief "type + dispatch" demo; Burn 3 is the **first iron session to run a program, return to the prompt, and keep typing** — exposing the sustained-spin wedge. `pic_mask_pit` removes the PIT variable entirely regardless of the exact dwell timing, so it is correct to ship even with this residual unresolved.

**FIX (behavioral, no instrumentation, prior-art-converged) — `pic_mask_pit()` in `pic.cyr`, called from `main.cyr` right after `apic_timer_init`.** Mask the PIT (IRQ0) at the master PIC once the LAPIC timer owns the timebase: master mask `0xFC → 0xFD` (IRQ0 masked, **IRQ1 keyboard stays live**). The LAPIC timer (INT32) never traverses the PIC, so the timebase is unaffected; early "ticks before sched" already come from the LAPIC (started line 87, counted line 2104). Placed while **IF=0** (the kernel's `sti` is far later) → no PIT IRQ0 can have been delivered/latched yet, so no unwedge is needed; a plain mask suffices. **Prior art (convergent):** Linux, xv6, and SeaBIOS all disable/mask the PIT when switching the timebase to the LAPIC timer — AGNOS simply kept the PIT for keyboard and forgot to mask IRQ0.

**Regression gate (QEMU — cannot reproduce the iron PIC path; no-regression only, all green on the fix):** production `build/agnos` **1,193,440 B** (+16 B vs .23's 1,193,424, the one call); `check.sh` **11/11`; `sweep.sh` PASS; `smp-smoke` **3/3** (`cpus online: 4`); `ring3-smoke` **8/8**; `agnsh-smoke` PASS (full banner + prompt). The fix is a true no-op in QEMU (USB keyboard path), as expected.

**`ls` (S2) is a SEPARATE kriya bug, NOT a wedge casualty (confirmed from source).** `kriya/src/cmd/ls.cyr:649`: with no operand (`npos < 1`), `cmd_ls` passes the **literal `"."`** to `_ls_list_dir`. AGNOS's VFS **hard-requires absolute paths** (`vfs.cyr:281-282` rejects any path whose first byte ≠ `/`), so `"."` can never open → `cmd_ls` returns a clean `EXIT_FAILURE` (no fault; child exits; shell survives — *"a subsequent command still worked"*). The QEMU delegation smoke only ever tested `ls /` (absolute), so this never surfaced. **Fix (kriya-side, one line):** when `npos < 1`, call `k_getcwd(buf, sz)` (`kriya/src/lib/sys.cyr:277-287` already returns `"/"` on agnos) and pass that absolute result instead of `"."`. Independent of the kernel fix — `pic_mask_pit` will NOT make bare `ls` work.

**`doom` (S3) IS the same wedge.** cyrius-doom blits the title (one frame — FB/blit#39 healthy), then `menu_run` polls `kbscan` (#42) which drains the **same `kb_buf` that IRQ1 feeds**. IRQ1 blocked → `kbscan` returns 0 forever → title renders once and waits for a key that never arrives = "freezes on the title card." Predicted to advance once keys flow post-fix.

**FREE discriminator (no new burn, no instrumentation — already in the binary):** the wedge predicts a **non-faulting live hang** — `CMOS[0x55] ≠ 0xE5` (no `idt` fault magic), `CMOS[0x54]`/CR2 stale, `CMOS[0x50] = 0x20` (ring-3 reached). If instead a fault family were responsible you'd see `0x55=0xE5` + a vector (`0x0E` #PF w/ CR2=`0xFEE000B0`, `0x0D` #GP, or `0x08` #DF). A `read-boot-log` after this burn confirms the non-faulting shape for free; the photo (no fault banner) already pre-confirms it.

**Re-burn falsification.**
- ✅ **PASS:** `help` renders AND the keyboard stays live afterward; multiple commands type + run repeatedly without input dying; `doom` advances past the title. Confirms the PIC wedge.
- ❌ **FALSIFIES (keyboard still dies after a program):** the PIT wasn't the (whole) wedge — pre-registered next suspects (chase ONLY then, do not pre-stack): a spurious-IRQ7/IRQ15 path needing a PIC EOI, or `kb_isr`'s non-specific `out 0x20,0x20` clearing the wrong in-service bit (→ specific-EOI the keyboard). Revert is the one `pic_mask_pit()` call (cannot regress — IRQ0 is dead weight once the LAPIC owns the timebase).
- `ls` listing `/`: requires the SEPARATE kriya one-liner; bare `ls` stays broken until it lands.

**Stacked burn-ready set (two one-liners, no instrumentation) — BOTH APPLIED + BUILT (user-approved 2026-06-13):** (1) `pic_mask_pit()` [agnos — fixes keyboard S1 + doom S3], cut as **agnos 1.44.24**. (2) kriya `cmd_ls` no-operand → `k_getcwd` (agnos-only via `#ifdef`, host byte-identical — `ls -R` section headers stay GNU-relative on Linux), cut as **kriya 1.1.3**; `kriya_agnos` rebuilt (933,864 B) + staged into `build/rootfs/bin`. The deferred large-arc item (re-enabling preemptive IF=1 agnsh via per-process kernel stacks) is NOT in this burn.

**Gate (2026-06-13):** agnos `build/agnos` **1,193,440 B**, banner now `v1.44.24`; `check.sh` 11/11 · `sweep.sh` PASS · `smp-smoke` 3/3 · `ring3-smoke` 8/8 · `agnsh-smoke` PASS. kriya host `smoke-ls.sh` 36/36 (the 2 transient `-R` failures from the first relative→absolute draft were fixed by gating the change behind `#ifdef CYRIUS_TARGET_AGNOS`).

**Burn delivery (two partitions):** the **kernel** (`pic_mask_pit`) rides `install-usb.sh --update` (ESP). The **`kriya_agnos`** binary lives on the agnos-fs **data partition**, which `--update` does NOT touch (ESP-only by design) — so the bare-`ls` fix needs a **mount-copy** of `agnos/build/rootfs/bin/kriya` → the disk's `/bin/kriya` ([[feedback_prefer_mount_modify_over_reflash]]). The keyboard fix (the burn-critical one) lands via `--update` alone.

NO auto-run per [[feedback_iron_burns_block_other_work]] — the user flashes + burns.

**🎯 IRON BURN 4 PREP (2026-06-13) — `pic_mask_pit` REFUTED; the burn that "tested" it ran a STALE artifact; the multi-verb keyboard-death is IRON-ONLY and bisects to the 1.44.18 SMP AP wake. Staged a single-core gate to test. NO burn run yet — this is the pre-burn hypothesis + falsification block.**

**Trigger.** User: the post-`pic_mask_pit` burn did NOT improve "more than one verb before frozen halt," and flagged a possible stale build. Investigation (no burn) established the following.

**1. The last burn was contaminated — a non-HEAD artifact (PROVEN, build-process failure, now fixed).** `build/agnos` is **deterministic**: committed HEAD (`e9eca4f`, = `pic_mask_pit` 1.44.24 + the dhcp repair) builds to **1,193,616 B / sha `6371acb5…`** twice, byte-identical. The artifact sitting on disk before the rebuild was **1,193,752 B / sha `3cebb132…`** — a *different* binary built from an uncommitted, since-discarded tree (working tree is clean; no `.cyr` newer than the artifact). So whatever was flashed last was **not the committed fix**. Per [[feedback_build_freshness_is_mine]] this is mine, not the user's (the user only flashes the drive). Consequence: **`pic_mask_pit` has never had a clean iron test** — its "failure" is uninterpretable.

**2. `pic_mask_pit` is REFUTED as the root cause (three independent ways).** (a) A PIT-IRQ0 8259 wedge is **permanent once latched** — with IRQ1 blocked, `kb_isr` never runs, so its EOI never clears IRQ0 → deadlock; that cannot explain `ls` leaving the keyboard **alive afterward** (Burn-3 report). (b) Under cooperative IF=0 agnsh the only IF=1 window is the blocking read held for the *whole line*, so a wedge would fire within one PIT tick (~10 ms) and kill the keyboard **mid-`help`** — but `help` typed and ran in full. (c) On archaemenid the firmware USB-legacy i8042 emulation was **already torn down** when AGNOS claimed xHCI + disabled the legacy SMI (the 1.41.13 finding, this log) — so there is likely no PIT-IRQ0 being delivered through the 8259 to mask in the first place. The Burn-3 root-cause's own "honest residual" (why did the keyboard work for two months on the same static PIC/APIC config) was the tell. **`pic_mask_pit` is a harmless no-op-or-near-no-op; it is NOT the fix. Do not re-burn it as the fix.**

**3. The bug is IRON-ONLY — it does NOT reproduce in QEMU even over the exact IRQ1 path (NEW reusable harness).** Built `agnos/scripts/agnsh-irq1-repro.py`: boots the production NVMe+ext2 image with **NO `usb-kbd`**, so HMP `sendkey` targets q35's PS/2 i8042 → **IRQ1 → `kb_isr` → `kb_buf`** — the *same code path* archaemenid uses (its firmware emulates an i8042 delivering via IRQ1). This is the path every prior smoke missed (they use `usb-kbd` → `hid_poll`, the QEMU-only path — which is exactly why this class is "invisible in QEMU"). Result: the 14115 sequence `help`→`version`→`mode` **all register cleanly**, even with `-smp 4` and a **30 s dwell** at the prompt (`SMP=4 DWELL=30`). So the IRQ1 path, the `kbd_read_blocking`+`preempt_disable`+`sti` read path, and the armed scheduler are all **logically correct**. The fault is real-Zen-specific, not AGNOS logic — which is also why a logic-only change like `pic_mask_pit` could never be validated by any smoke. NOT a PS/2-on-iron suggestion ([[feedback_no_ps2_suggestions]]) — QEMU's i8042 is purely a test stand-in for the IRQ1 *code path*.

**4. The bisect.** Iron multi-verb keyboard **worked** at burn 14115 (1.41.15) and burn 1439 (DOOM playable, 1.43.8) — both PRE-1.44.x. **RULED OUT** as the regression: `sched_active=1` arming (dates to 2026-04-06, two months before the working burns — not new); the 1.44.0 `sched_active=0`→`preempt_disable` read-path swap (both just no-op `do_context_switch` during the read — functionally inert). The one 1.44.x change that is **genuinely iron-specific, unmodelable in QEMU, and the log's own flagged "RISKIEST ITEM"** is the **SMP AP wake (1.44.18)** — the first time AGNOS actively wakes archaemenid's 3 other Zen cores (pre-1.44.18 they were never woken). `ap_entry` (`smp.cyr:48`) enables each AP's LAPIC (`SVR=0x1FF`) but **never masks its LVT** (LINT0/LINT1/timer), relying on INIT having reset them. **Hypothesis:** on real Zen a woken-but-parked AP competes for / swallows the BSP's 8259 IRQ1 (keyboard) — probabilistic per keystroke, fitting the `help`-dead / `ls`-alive shape and "frozen after the first command."

SMP buys NOTHING for cooperative-agnsh boot-to-shell (the MVP gate), so gating the wake is BOTH the discriminator AND — if confirmed — the MVP fix. Flip `smp_wake_enabled`→1 to restore (the documented 1.44.18 one-line fallback; everything downstream is inert without it). Rides on the 1.44.24 base, **uncut** (no version bump per [[feedback_no_unprompted_version_bumps]]).

**🎯 IRON BURN 5 RESULT + THE FIX — busy-spin → `hlt`-park (`kbd_read_blocking`). Photo `iron-nuc-zen-photos/14423-agnos-1.44.23-burn5-rb2-hang-keyboard-dies-after-cmd1.jpg` (2026-06-13 20:44).** The marker burn settled the 3-way: `[rb1>|h||e|l||l|p|||` `ent]` → help renders fully → `[ASSIST] > [rb2>|` → **hard-hung** (user-confirmed: wedged *before any input*). The trailing `|` is **not a keystroke** — it's the residual Enter-**break** (release of the Enter that submitted `help`) draining as read#2's first scancode. So: **agnsh reached read#2 (exonerated, not its own dispatch); `scancode_to_ascii`/echo exonerated (read#1 exercised them fully); the wedge is the read#2 wait itself.**

- **Audit (the full `1.41.15..HEAD` delta, re-derived, no burns):** xHCI/`hid_poll` is **byte-identical to 14115 + spec-correct + FreeBSD/Haiku/EDK2-converged** → not it; `apic.cyr` delta is SMP-only → inert (gated); `pic_mask_pit` masks IRQ0 only (can't kill the LAPIC timebase) + post-dates the bug → not it; RSP0=0x3C0000 has **1.6 MB clearance** over image-end 0x233688 → not reintroduced; `do_context_switch` is double-gated (`sched_active` *and* `preempt_count≥1` for the whole read) + `preempt_enable` underflow-protected → no spurious switch. **The kbd_read_blocking diff 1.41.15→HEAD is functionally just `sched_active=0` → `preempt_disable()` (equivalent — only comments sit between the two `do_context_switch` returns).** Frozen-NOT-rebooted (real HW reboots on `#DF→#DF`) ⟹ it's a **loop**, not a fault: stuck in `while(kb_has_key()==0){}` ⟹ **IRQ1 delivery stopped after command 1.**
- **Root cause (matches the §165/§180 pre-registered suspect #2 exactly): the firmware USB-legacy i8042-emulation SMM path stalls under *sustained non-drain*.** `kbd_read_blocking` was the ONLY kernel waiter that holds IF=1 and **busy-spins** in ring-0 across many ticks; the proven-on-iron waiters do NOT — `sleep_ms`#41 `hlt`-parks (DOOM frame pacing) and `kbscan`#42 uses brief bounded windows (DOOM input, **iron-confirmed at burn `1439`**). A never-yielding CPU spin starves the firmware's periodic-SMI scancode injection → keyboard dies after the first line. Invisible in QEMU/KVM (no SMM USB-legacy emulation — both pass `help`→`version`→`mode` over IRQ1 *and* `hid_poll`, TCG *and* `-cpu host`).
- **FIX (1-line behavioral, `core/syscall.cyr`):** `while (kb_has_key()==0) { }` → `while (kb_has_key()==0) { arch_wait(); }` (`hlt`). Adopts the proven `sleep_ms`#41 recipe (sti + `arch_wait`, preempt-disabled so the timer ticks+EOIs but never switches); `hlt` yields the pipeline so SMM runs; IF=1 so the 100 Hz timer guarantees a wake and a keystroke's own IRQ1 wakes `hlt` immediately. Blocking semantics + the `[rbN>`/`|`/`ent]` markers preserved (kept as the success indicator for this burn; strip once iron-confirmed). `build/agnos` **1,193,976 B**, multiboot2 OK. Rides `install-usb.sh --update` (ESP, kernel-only — no agnos-fs mount-copy).

**🎯 IRON BURN 6 PREP + THE REAL FIX — keyboard transfer-ring depth 1→16 + read-entry re-kick (`usb/hid.cyr` + `core/syscall.cyr`). NOT yet burned. The burn-5 `hlt` fix was burned and FALSIFIED the SMM-non-drain theory (user: "no improvement" — keyboard still died after command 1), so busy-spin-starvation is dead.** Re-derived from source with the input path finally **SETTLED** (user-confirmed: archaemenid is **USB-keyboard-ONLY** — no PS/2, no i8042, so IRQ1/`kb_isr`/PIC is **dead code on this box**; keystrokes arrive via the xHCI `hid_poll` ring; [[project_archaemenid_keystroke_path_usb_only]]). The §178 IRQ1-vs-`hid_poll` fork is closed: it's `hid_poll`. Burns 3-5 (`pic_mask_pit`, SMP-LVT, `hlt`-park) all chased the dead IRQ1/PIC/SMM path.

- **Polling is NOT the problem.** At read#2 the spin `while (kb_has_key()==0)` calls `hid_poll()` every iteration (syscall.cyr:296, IF=1) — the ring is drained constantly. The keyboard interrupt endpoint itself has **stopped producing transfer events** during the preceding IF=0 command-execution gap (ring 3 runs IF=0 between syscalls; `hid_poll` only runs from the read spin).
- **It's endpoint-specific, NOT a global ring/SMM desync.** MSC storage (ext2/fat) rides the SAME shared event ring via `xhci_wait_transfer_event` and works throughout boot + commands → the firmware is not stealing/desyncing the ring. The fault is specific to the keyboard's PERIODIC interrupt-IN endpoint (must stay continuously armed), not the on-demand MSC endpoint.
- **Root cause = ring-exhaustion EP stall — the SAME class `hid.cyr:247-258` already documents (the 1.33.4 interactive-lockup).** The kbd transfer ring was armed exactly **one TRB deep** (`hid_kbd_configure`). During the gap the lone TRB is consumed by a key release, the controller hits the empty ring at the next service interval and **stalls the endpoint** — and a bare re-arm+doorbell does not reliably restart a stalled interrupt EP on real xHCI. Different trigger from 1.33.4 (gap-depletion vs running off the page end), identical cure: keep the ring from emptying.
- **FIX (kernel-only).** (1) `hid_kbd_configure` arms the transfer ring **16 TRBs deep** (was 1); `hid_poll` keeps it topped up (arm-1-per-event conserves depth) — 16 ≫ the ≤1 report a builtin's gap can consume (1 report/bInterval, gap is ms), so the controller always finds a valid TRB and never stalls. All TRBs share `report_buf`; a gap coalescing >1 report keeps only the last (the resting key state) — applied once, clean. (2) new `hid_kbd_kick()` rings the kbd doorbell at the START of every `kbd_read_blocking` — restarts a stalled-but-armed EP (spec no-op if Running). (3) killed the false "IRQ1 fills kb_buf on iron" comment in syscall.cyr that misdirected burns 3-5. `build/agnos` **1,194,296 B**, multiboot2 OK. Rides `install-usb.sh --update` (ESP, kernel-only — no agnos-fs mount-copy).
- **QEMU gate (all green — QEMU's usb-kbd can't model the stall, so this is no-regression ONLY, not proof of the iron fix):** `check.sh` **11/11** · `agnsh-smoke` PASS · `agnsh-type-test` multi-command (`version`/`mode` across read#2/#3/#4, keystrokes flow) PASS · `doom-smoke` PASS (240 colors — same `hid_poll`/`kbscan` path I deepened) · `ext2-smoke` **5/5** (MSC intact — USB code touched).
- **Hypothesis + falsification (this burn):** type `help` ↵ then `version` ↵ then `mode` ↵. ✅ **PASS = the fix:** after command 1, `[rb2>` shows `|` marks as you type command 2 and it executes; multi-command typing survives like 14115. ❌ **FALSIFIES the ring-exhaustion theory:** still hard-hangs at `[rb2>` with no `|` on a real keypress ⟹ depth+kick is insufficient — the EP needs a full **Reset-Endpoint + Set-TR-Dequeue-Pointer** restart (the next concrete step, NOT a guess). HEAD = working tree (uncommitted; user owns git). NO auto-run per [[feedback_iron_burns_block_other_work]].

**Arc.** The deep cooperative→preemptive scheduler transition ([[project_multithreading_future_arc]]): interrupt-safe context save/restore, reentrant-or-gated syscalls, preemptive ring-3 time-slicing, concurrent `spawn`+`waitpid` (DOOM while the shell stays live), SMP-AP wakeup. Multi-patch line; absorbs the 1.43.x carry-forward (per-process env, FB scaling, zero-copy FB-mmap; first-`mmap` RIP=0 already repaired at 1.43.8).

**Opening kernel bite (agnos 1.44.0) — QEMU-GREEN (`agnos/scripts/thread-smoke.sh`).**
- ✅ **`kthread_create`** (`core/proc.cyr`) — a schedulable kernel thread on the shared AS (CR3 0x1000, IF=1) from a static stack pool; generalizes the hand-rolled 1.40.10 idle proc into a spawn API.
- ✅ **Preempt gate** (`core/proc.cyr` + `core/sched.cyr`) — `preempt_count` + `preempt_disable`/`preempt_enable`, wired into `do_context_switch` (a timer tick with `preempt_count>0` is a no-op switch). The "reentrant-or-gated" foundation that lets the single-core no-lock invariant (shared scratch buffers, FS code) survive preemption; formalizes the ad-hoc syscall-path guard.
- ✅ **`THREAD_SELFTEST`** — two kthreads tight-loop bumping counters (never yielding); both reach the **tens of millions** while the timer round-robins them (`thr: preempt OK` — preemptive time-slicing PROVEN), and under `preempt_disable()` the counters **freeze** (`thr: gate held`). `sweep.sh` **7/7** (production `do_context_switch` byte-identical — the gate is inert at `preempt_count=0`).
- **Next bites:** per-process-AS preemptive ring-3 time-slicing (different-CR3 mid-slice) · the FS-scratch reentrancy audit/gating · `kthread_yield` · concurrent ring-3 `spawn`+`waitpid` · SMP-AP wakeup.
- **Iron rubric (rides the next burn):** `thr: preempt OK` + `thr: gate held` on real Zen (the timer-IRQ context-switch path was iron-proven since Attempt 16; this adds the gate + the spawn API on top, so the iron risk is low). Falsifies if a kthread starves (no round-robin) or the gate leaks (counters advance under `preempt_disable`) on hardware.

**Ring-3 parent `spawn`+`waitpid` (agnos 1.44.10) — QEMU-GREEN (`agnos/scripts/ring3-smoke.sh`, 4/4).**
- ✅ **`spawn`#3 runs `elf_load` under the kernel CR3** (`core/syscall.cyr`) — the deferred 1.44.9 blocker, cleared. A ring-3 parent's per-process CR3 mirrors only 0–256 MB + the GB ranges; the child's fresh page-table pages (allocated anywhere in phys) could land in the unmapped 256 MB–1 GB gap → bytes loaded right but tables mis-wired → #UD-at-child-entry triple-fault. Fix mirrors the `execwait`#37 idiom: copy the user ELF to a 16 KB kernel staging buffer (`spawn_elf_buf`) while the caller CR3 resolves `arg1` (SMAP AC=1), switch to boot CR3 `0x1000` (identity-maps all phys → every PT page reachable, the proven 1.44.8 condition), `elf_load`, restore the caller CR3. Kernel callers (the selftest's proc B) don't reach this path.
- ✅ **`RING3_SELFTEST` proc P** — a position-independent (RIP-relative) raw-machine-code parent embeds a minimal exit-only child ELF inline, `spawn`(#3)s it, poll-`waitpid`(#4)s it to exit, then `exit(0xABCD)`. Success is the parent's **exit code** (verify reads `proc_get_exit_code` — kernel-side, no fragile user-page mapping under the parent CR3). New assert: **`ring3: parent spawn+wait OK`** (`Pcode=0xABCD`, child pid 5 spawned + run + reaped, all from ring 3).
- **Iron rubric (rides the next burn):** the 4 `ring3:` PASS lines on real Zen, esp. `parent spawn+wait OK`. Iron risk low — the ring-3 exec + timer-IRQ context-switch paths are already iron-proven; this adds the kernel-CR3 spawn copy on top. Falsifies if the child #UDs (mis-wired tables) or the parent never observes the exit on hardware.
- **Build-side facts surfaced (Cyrius 6.0.56, not AGNOS bugs):** (1) two adjacent module-global fill arrays (`spawn_child_elf[24]` + `spawn_parent_payload[512]`) physically **overlapped** — `&parent` landed only 32 B into `child`'s region — so `child`'s fill clobbered `parent`; folded to one array to sidestep it. **CORRECTION (1.44.11):** this was a placement quirk, NOT a unit issue — a module-global `var X[N]` is **N·u64 = 8N bytes** (confirmed: `ext2.cyr:44-50`, e.g. `ext2_bgdt_buf[512]` = 4096 B; matches CLAUDE.md + [[cyrius-var-array-u64-units]]). An earlier note here mis-stated "~N bytes" — that was a misdiagnosis (the later `dspawn_payload[512]` crash was the page-table VA collision, not a memset overflow). (2) `exit` RETURNS to ring 3 (scheduler retires the state-0 proc next tick), so a ring-3 payload must `jmp $`-spin after its `exit` syscall.

**Page-table VA-collision fix (agnos 1.44.11, cut 2026-06-10) — QEMU-GREEN (`ring3-smoke` 5/5).**
- ✅ **Foundational multi-proc unlock.** The in-memory loaders (`spawn_user_proc` code `0x400000+pid*0x200000` / stack `0x800000+pid*0x400000`; `elf_load` same stack) put 2 MB USER pages at ≥16 MB (PD[8..63]), **overwriting** `proc_create_address_space`'s identity-SUPERVISOR map of the pmm pool — so a kernel `load64(PML4)` in `proc_get_user_cr3` **SMAP-faulted (#PF cpl=0 e=1)** on the next context switch once the pmm pushed a page-table page physically into 16–128 MB (i.e. ≥~6 coexisting ring-3 procs). Diagnosed via `-d int` (CR2=0xfe4000, a corrupted CR3 in PD[8]) + a 4-agent scope workflow.
- ✅ **Fix:** de-stripe to FIXED user VAs outside PD[8..63] — code `0x400000` (PD[2]), stack `0x3FC00000` (PD[510]) — mirroring `elf_load_from_file` (which already dodged it); own-CR3 isolation makes a fixed VA safe. Coupled mandatory `elf_load` `pmm_alloc`→`pmm_alloc_2mb` (2 MB huge-page alignment). Guard-page unmaps self-guard at `0x3FA00000` (not-present arena slot).
- ✅ **Validated:** `ring3-smoke` **5/5** with a new `≥8`-concurrent-proc `stress OK` assert — **before/after proven**: striped triple-faults at scheduler activation (no output), de-striped all live. `exec-smoke` 9/9, `sweep` 7/7, `check.sh` 11/11. **Unblocks `spawn_path`#43** (non-blocking from-disk spawn, staged dead-code; awaits a non-LIFO reap + the prompt-preempt decision). Iron rides the next burn (low risk — the boot-path `elf_load`/`spawn_user_proc` change is QEMU + exec-smoke covered; iron-validated boot-to-shell uses fixed VAs that pass `is_user_ptr`).

**Non-LIFO proc-table-slot reclaim (agnos 1.44.12, cut 2026-06-10) — QEMU-GREEN (`ring3-smoke` 7/7).**
- ✅ **The next concurrent-procs blocker.** `proc_reap`/`proc_reap_child` freed the address space + pages and marked the slot `state==0`, but reclaimed the slot INDEX only LIFO — so an out-of-order background-job exit left a dead hole that append-only `proc_create`/`proc_create_full` never reused → the 16-slot table leaks under bg-job churn. Scoped via a 2-agent blast-radius + validation workflow.
- ✅ **Fix:** new `proc_alloc_slot()` scans `[0,proc_count)` for the first `state==0` slot + REUSES it (else appends, cap 16); both create paths call it. Mandatory companion (the regression reuse exposes): it clears the recycled slot's `proc_signals`/`proc_sigmask` (cleared on neither create nor reap before — a recycled slot would inherit stale pending signals). LIFO collapse kept as an optimization; `sched_next` already skips holes.
- ✅ **Validated:** `ring3-smoke` **7/7** — new `nonlifo reuse OK` (a non-top reaped slot REUSED by the next spawn; **before/after proven**: append-only → `C=4, pc grows`, FIXED → `C=2` reused) + `nonlifo signal clear OK`. **Foreground iron path byte-identical** (`reap: slot reclaim OK (pc stable)` green — sequential exec is create-top→run→LIFO-collapse, no hole). `exec-smoke` 9/9, `sweep` 7/7, `check.sh` 11/11, production 1,141,840 B. PID-RECYCLE + SMP caveats documented in the `proc_alloc_slot` header. Iron rides the next burn.

**Schedulable-agnsh ladder, 1.44.13–17 (all cut 2026-06-10) — QEMU-GREEN; per-cut detail → agnos CHANGELOG.**
- ✅ **1.44.13** spawn_path#43 validated + the non-blocking cooked-line read · **1.44.14** schedulable agnsh WORKS (preemptive agnsh via `exec_preempt` IF=1 + `exec_resume_pid` exit-gate + -3 partial-line; agnoshi 1.6.0/`&`) · **1.44.15** multi-job out-of-order reap validated · **1.44.16** `sched_yield`#44 (abandon-frame; bg ×1.78) · **1.44.17** idle-deprioritization (cumulative bg ×2.3, 22.8s→9.8s). agnoshi at **1.6.1**.

**SMP-AP wake + park (agnos 1.44.18, cut 2026-06-11) — QEMU-GREEN (`smp-smoke` 3/3). THE RISKIEST ITEM ON THE NEXT BURN.**
- ✅ **What landed:** the arc's last parked item un-gated — INIT-SIPI-SIPI (SDM/Linux/SeaBIOS/xv6 shape) with REAL tick-timed delays wakes APs 1-3; each runs the 16→32→64 trampoline → `ap_entry` (LAPIC enable, APIC-ID, per-CPU TSS, spinlock'd count-in, idempotence mask) → **PARKS (IF=0 hlt)**. No AP ever takes an interrupt or runs the scheduler — the single-core invariant is untouched. Two latent never-executed trampoline bugs fixed (kernel-GDT-borrow → 32-bit stage decoded as 16-bit, AP crash; stale GDT limit 55 → `ltr` #GP) + `ap_entry` made invariant-safe (was arming the AP timer + sti).
- ✅ **QEMU:** `smp-smoke` 3/3 (`-smp 4` → `smp: cpus online: 4` + scheduler + kybernet); the FULL battery green at `-smp 1` (absent APs never respond; ~120 ms bounded waits, no fault).
- ⏳ **IRON hypothesis (next burn):** archaemenid Zen wakes ≥3 APs through the real INIT-SIPI timing and prints `smp: cpus online: 4`, then boots to agnsh normally. **Falsification:** (a) no `smp: cpus online` line / count < 4 → real-Zen IPI timing or trampoline issue (the tick delays may need lengthening; SDM minimums are generous but Zen firmware state differs from QEMU's clean APs); (b) boot freezes between "Timer ticks before sched" and "Activating scheduler" → an AP triple-faulted (trampoline/GDT path) — REVERT = re-gate the INIT-SIPI block in `smp_start_aps` (one-line; everything else is inert without it); (c) boot proceeds but later hangs → a parked AP took an interrupt it shouldn't (check LAPIC LVT state). The burn also carries the whole 1.44.x preemptive stack (preemptive agnsh, #43/#44, bg jobs) — if the burn must isolate, re-gate SMP first (it's the only new pre-scheduler boot-path code). **1.44.21 fail-safe update:** the APIC INIT/SIPI delivery-status polls (formerly unbounded `while`-spins, made live by THIS wake) are now bounded (`ICR_DELIVERY_WAIT`, `apic.cyr`), so a stuck IPI whose delivery-status never clears now **bounds-out and continues the boot** (a non-responding AP simply never checks in) instead of hanging — this narrows falsification (a)/the hang risk to an AP that triple-faults *inside* the trampoline (b), which the one-line re-gate fallback still covers. Byte-identical on the success path.

- ✅ **1.44.20 kernel-scaled blit** — #39 a4[39:32] integer scale; the adversarial review caught a draft BSS smash → the `w*scale ≤ 8192` gate is THE safety invariant. cyrius-doom 0.29.0 drops its userland scaler.
- ✅ **1.44.21 arc-closing security + iron hardening** (multi-dimension audit of `1.43.8..HEAD`; arc found solid) — (1) the `kbd_read_nonblock` cross-call **OOB-flush clamp**: the line-flush copied the PERSISTENT accumulator (`nbline_pos` bytes) into the current call's user buffer bounded only by a PRIOR call's `count` → ring-3-reachable OOB write / kernel #PF DoS; both flush paths now clamp to THIS call's validated `count` (byte-identical for agnsh's fixed prompt count). (2) the APIC delivery-status timeout (above). nbread 4/4 · ring3 8/8 · agnsh-bg · smp 3/3 · sweep 7/7 · check 11/11.

One burn of 1.44.22 validates the whole arc.

**Userland testing surface (the concurrent-tools story this arc unlocks) — coreutils delegation, also QEMU-GREEN.**

**Scope.** agnoshi **1.5.0** removed its 12 in-process FS verbs (the 1.42.6 stopgap "until ring-3 execwait") + `src/verbs.cyr`; agnsh is now a pure launcher. A bareword `cp`/`mv`/`rm`/`ls`/`mkdir`/`rmdir`/`touch`/`echo`/`wc`/`find`/`grep` falls through `sh_try_bareword_launch` → `/bin/<verb>` (a symlink → the **kriya** dispatcher) → `execwait`#37; `cat` is nudged to **owl** (no `/bin/cat`). kriya (`kriya_agnos`) + owl (`owl_agnos`) are staged onto the agnos-fs `/bin` with 11 `/bin/<verb>` → `kriya` relative symlinks (`agnos/scripts/stage-tools.sh`). Kernel needs no new syscall (`symlink`#43 for kriya `ln -s` is deferred to backlog).

**Hypothesis.** With the in-process verbs removed, a bareword `cp`/`ls`/`rm`/`echo`/`owl FILE` on agnsh routes through the `/bin` launcher to the staged kriya/owl binaries (the ext2 open path follows the `/bin/<verb>` symlink inode to the dispatcher, which routes on `basename(argv[0])`), producing correct FS effects on the ext2 root — proving the shell-as-launcher model end to end on iron.

**QEMU validation (`agnos/scripts/agnsh-delegation-test.py`, headless HMP `sendkey`, 2026-06-09).** Self-builds an image seeded from `build/rootfs` (`/bin` + symlinks) + a `/hello.txt`, boots the real 1.44.0 kernel, drives barewords. Results:
- ✅ **Delegation mechanism works** — `mkdir /dtest`, `touch /tfile`, `cp /hello.txt /h2` each exec via the `/bin/<verb>` → kriya symlink and return to a clean `[ASSIST] >` prompt (no NL-intent fallback, no error) → symlink-follow-on-open + argv delivery (argc=3 for cp) + kriya dispatch all confirmed.
- ✅ **`cat` → owl nudge** prints `cat: AGNOS reads files with owl -- try 'owl FILE' ...` (agnsh 1.5.0).
- ✅ **kriya `ls`/`find`/`du` — FIXED at kriya 1.1.2.** `ls /` initially hung. Bisected on the iron-path via FB markers to **`vec_push`** (NOT getdents — kriya's `src/lib/fs.cyr` already translates the agnos packed-dirent layout correctly): kriya's vendored `lib/fnptr.cyr` (cyrius pin **6.0.56**, pre-6.1.14) had **no `CYRIUS_TARGET_AGNOS` fncall branch**, so `alloc_via` (the allocator vtable, dispatched through `fncallN`) returned 0 on agnos → `vec_new`/`vec_push` produced null-backed vecs → `ls` infinite-looped on its first push. Fix: **cyrius pin 6.0.56 → 6.1.14 + re-vendor `lib/`** (same class as agnoshi 1.4.9). `ls /` now lists `bin`/`hello.txt`/`lost+found` and returns. (My first guess — a getdents Linux-layout bug in the *stdlib* `lib/fs.cyr` — was wrong: that file isn't on ls's path.)
- ✅ **owl `-p FILE` — FIXED at owl 1.3.8.** Two AGNOS-only bugs: (1) `var r = main();` at module scope ran `main` during gvar-init, *before* cycc's init-rsp capture (cyrius 6.1.14 moved it after gvar-inits), so `argc()`==0 → owl saw no positional → `print_help()`; fixed with the canonical bare top-level call (`_owl_entry();`). (2) the same stale-`fnptr.cyr` allocator gap (pin 6.0.56 → 6.1.14 + re-vendor). `owl -p /hello.txt` now prints the file content.

**Scope.** The 1.41.x arc moved the interactive shell out of the kernel: **1.41.4** PID-1 `kybernet` execs `/bin/agnsh` (the `cyrius build --agnos` agnoshi binary) in ring 3 off the ext2 root, with the in-kernel `shell()` as the fallback; **1.41.7** added the `VFS_SEC_WFILE` write-fd so agnsh can write FAT/exFAT data partitions via `open(AO_WRONLY)`+`write`+`close`; **1.41.8/1.41.9** shrank the in-kernel shell to a recovery REPL (`shell.cyr` 1149→813 LOC); **1.41.5/1.41.6/1.41.10** hardened the ring-3→ring-0 surface (epoll/timerfd type-confusion, the 1 GB user-VA ceiling, ELF/mmap/spawn bounds, the write-fd pool-leak DoS + write-cap spin + directory-overwrite corruption). This burn validates the whole arc is byte-valid on real Zen, the same dispositive bar (boot-to-prompt + host-`fsck` clean) as the 1.40.x exec burn.

**Hypothesis.** On archaemenid, the production kernel (1.41.11) with `/bin/agnsh` seeded on the ext2 root will (a) reach the **agnsh prompt in ring 3** — `kybernet: exec /bin/agnsh` → `agnoshi 1.3.x / AI-native shell…` to the **FB** (iron-readable `kprint`; no serial — [[feedback_no_serial_on_iron]]), no `#UD`, no emergency-shell fallback; (b) fall back to the **in-kernel recovery `agnos>`** shell (with `cat`/`ls`/`run` working) if `/bin/agnsh` is renamed/absent; (c) **write a FAT/exFAT data-partition file** from agnsh that survives a power-cycle and is **host-`fsck` clean**; (d) show **no regression** in the exec/storage/net stack (the box still rides past `Activating scheduler...` → kybernet, as the 1.40.x arc is iron-validated).

**Falsification criteria.** `#UD`/`#PF` at agnsh startup or a fallback to the emergency shell when `/bin/agnsh` is present → the cyrius `CYRIUS_TARGET_AGNOS` stdlib peer or the kernel exec path differs on Zen vs QEMU (re-check `args_agnos` init-stack capture, the ring-3 enter path). Recovery `agnos>` not reachable with `/bin/agnsh` absent → the shrunk shell's fallback path regressed. FAT/exFAT write not `fsck`-clean → the write-fd flush (`vfs_write_on` → `fatfs_write_file`/`exfat_write_file`) writes differently on real NAND. A reset before kybernet → an exec/scheduler regression vs the iron-validated 1.40.x baseline.

#### 1.41.12 ring-3 stdout FB-mirror — burn `14111_stallout` diagnosed + fixed (2026-06-04)

**Burn `14111_stallout` (the arc's first hardware touch).** Boot rode clean: AHCI WD Blue + GPT (3 parts: ESP / agnos-fs / `fstest`) → `VFS initialized` → `ext2: mounted` (blocksize=4096) + `jbd2: clean journal seq=10` + `fat: mounted FAT32` → `SYSCALL/SYSRET` + stack-canary + `Interrupts enabled` → `Activating scheduler...` → DHCP **real lease** (`ACK ip=192.168.1.159 gw=192.168.1.1`, `arp REPLY gw d4:6a:91:ce:70:60`, `net: L2 OK`) → `Launching kybernet` → `kybernet: 2 processes` / `2950 free pages` → **`kybernet: exec /bin/agnsh`** — then the FB froze. **A4 (no exec/storage/net regression) ✓** — rides past `Activating scheduler` intact (the 1.40.10 scheduler-reset baseline holds). **No `#UD`, no reset, no `emergency shell`.**

**Diagnosis — NOT a hang; ring-3 stdout was serial-only.** The user's read ("interesting it didn't fault and drop to emergency") trisected it: a clean exec-failure return → `emergency shell` (didn't fire); a bad-ELF jump → `#PF`→triple-fault→reset (would've rebooted, didn't); ⇒ silent. A 6-reader parallel audit + the QEMU `agnsh-smoke` **serial** log (which prints the full `agnoshi 1.3.5` banner right after the exec line) proved agnsh **loads** (69-block ext2 single-indirect read ✓), **enters ring 3** ✓, and **prints its banner** ✓ — but `write(1,…)` → `vfs_write` → `dev_write(0)` → `serial_dev_write` → `serial_print` **only, no fb**. Serial-less archaemenid ([[feedback_no_serial_on_iron]]) → banner invisible → FB frozen at the last `kprint` line. **Regression** from the 1.41.x write-surface refactor (1.40.9 had ring-3 `write(1)`→`kputc/fb`, iron-visible — see the 1.40.x tracker `EXEC-DISK-OK`/`run: exit 42/90`). Exonerated by the QEMU banner: ext2 indirect read, KPTI page-map, `mmap`.

**Fix (1.41.12, behavioral).** `serial_dev_write` → `kprint` (serial+fb mirror, `core/devs.cyr`). agnsh-smoke **PASS**, banner retained on serial, build **1,070,704 B**. **Next-burn A1 is now OBSERVABLE** — expect the agnsh banner on the **FB**, then agnsh blocks on `read(fd 0)`→`kbd_read_blocking`. (The original note here predicted "no keystrokes, next blocker = a separate xhci USB-HID arc." That framing was WRONG — see the typing sub-entry below; the keyboard works on iron via IRQ1/i8042, the xHCI ring being empty is a red herring.)

#### agnsh typing on iron — `kbd_read_blocking` ran interrupts-masked (burn `14112` @ 1.41.12 → fix @ 1.41.13, 2026-06-05)

**Burn `14112_140_boot_no_typing` result.** agnsh reached the `[ASSIST] >` prompt on the FB (the 1.41.12 PMM top-down fix cleared the ring-3 `#PF`, so A1 is now FB-confirmed: `agnoshi 1.4.0` banner + prompt). **But typing produced nothing** at the prompt — the open item the previous note mis-attributed to "USB-HID never worked on iron."

**Diagnosis (corrected — the earlier "empty kbd ring" comment was the misdirection).** The keyboard works on archaemenid: the in-kernel recovery shell types fine on iron. There is no PS/2 hardware; keystrokes arrive as **i8042 scancodes from the firmware's UEFI USB-legacy emulation**, delivered via **IRQ1** to `kb_isr`. The xHCI USB-HID event ring that `hid_poll()` drains stays empty on this box (firmware owns the device) — but that is NOT "keyboard broken." The agnsh path failed for a different reason: `read(fd 0)`→`kbd_read_blocking` runs **with interrupts masked** (SYSCALL clears IF via SFMASK=0x200, `syscall_hw.cyr:98`), so the IRQ1 ISR could never fire, and it only polled the (empty) xHCI ring → hung at a visible prompt. The in-kernel shell typed fine because it runs with IF=1, letting IRQ1 deliver.

**Fix (1.41.13, behavioral, `kbd_read_blocking` + new `kb_poll_i8042`).** Keep interrupts masked (an `sti` here lets the xHCI raise an interrupt the kernel only polls — a QEMU **baseline regression** confirmed this), and instead **poll the i8042 data port directly** (ports 0x60/0x64, bounded ≤16, AUXB-skip) alongside the existing `hid_poll()`. On a given box exactly one source has data, so draining both is race-free. **QEMU-validated:** `agnsh-type-test.py` (xHCI usb-kbd) PASS — `help`/`version`/`mode` register cleanly, no regression, no doubling (the i8042 poll is a no-op in QEMU since `sendkey` targets the usb-kbd). Build **1,070,976 B**.

**Hypothesis (this burn).** At the `[ASSIST] >` prompt, typing now echoes and commands run — the firmware's USB-legacy i8042 emulation answers polled reads of port 0x60 (standard behavior; it is how a bootloader reads a USB keyboard), so `kb_poll_i8042()` retrieves scancodes with IF masked.

**Falsification criteria.** Still no keystrokes at the prompt → the firmware emulation presents scancodes ONLY on IRQ1 (not on polled OBF), OR it withholds the byte until the IRQ is ack'd. Fallback: **selectively unmask IRQ1 at the PIC** (mask everything else, no broad `sti`) so the proven ISR path fires without enabling the xHCI interrupt that broke QEMU; if that also fails, mask the LAPIC timer + `sti` (IRQ1-only wake) as the next step. A stray/garbage char after a line → the pending-IRQ1-latched-during-IF=0 edge (a known minor; mask IRQ1 at the PIC during the read to close it). **QEMU cannot exercise this path** (it uses a real usb-kbd → hid_poll), so this burn is the only validation.

**Read-out (FB):** type `help` at `[ASSIST] >`. PASS = the help text prints. Falsifies = no echo, nothing.

#### agnsh typing on iron — 1.41.13's i8042-poll FALSIFIED by a 2nd identical burn → corrected at 1.41.14 (2026-06-05)

**Burn result (user, 2nd time).** Flashed 1.41.13 (the polled-`kb_poll_i8042` fix). Same as the prior burn: `[ASSIST] >` prompt on the FB, **no keystrokes accepted or echoed** (photo `14112_140_boot_no_typing.jpg`, identical to the pre-1.41.13 burn). So **1.41.13's hypothesis was wrong** — the firmware does NOT present USB-legacy scancodes on polled `0x60`/`0x64` on archaemenid.

**Why 1.41.13 was wrong (root cause, now nailed).** AGNOS's xHCI bring-up **claims the controller from BIOS** (USBLEGSUP HC-OS-Owned) and **disables the legacy SMI** (`usb/xhci_port.cyr` `xhci_usblegctlsts_disable_smi`, mirroring Linux `quirk_usb_handoff_xhci`). That teardown is exactly what kills the firmware's SMM-based USB-legacy i8042 emulation — so polled `0x60`/`0x64` reads open-bus `0xFF`, and 1.41.13 drained nothing. (It passed `agnsh-type-test` only because QEMU has a *real* PS/2 i8042 and the poll was a no-op there.) This is what [[feedback_no_ps2_suggestions]] codifies: the i8042 path is a dead end on this hardware.

**The actual fault layer (user was right — it's the agnsh read path, NOT the keyboard driver).** The keyboard *driver* is correct and proven on iron: the in-kernel recovery shell types via IRQ1 → `kb_isr` → `kb_buf` with IF=1. agnsh's `read(fd 0)` → `kbd_read_blocking` is the only failing path, and the **frozen ABI itself** (`agnos-userland-abi.md` §3.1) specified its mechanism as "busy-poll `hid_poll()` with **interrupts MASKED**" — a QEMU-only assumption. On iron, keystrokes arrive only via IRQ1, which IF-masking structurally blocks. Five sessions chased the keyboard *driver* (xHCI HID enum, i8042) because of garbage "iron-state-as-fact" comments asserting the firmware-emulation theory.

**Fix (1.41.14, behavioral, the right layer).** `kbd_read_blocking` now re-enables interrupts (`sti`) for the blocking read so IRQ1 → `kb_isr` fills `kb_buf` — **exactly how the in-kernel shell types on this box**. Safe in a non-reentrant syscall because preemption is suspended (`sched_active=0`): the timer ISR still EOIs + ticks but `do_context_switch` early-returns. The dead `kb_poll_i8042` is removed; `hid_poll()` still drains the xHCI ring for QEMU. The garbage comments (`boot_finish.cyr`/`syscall.cyr`/`keyboard.cyr`) + the ABI §3.1 mechanism clause are corrected. **The in-kernel shell already proves IRQ1 → `kb_isr` works on archaemenid with IF=1 + the same xHCI MSI-X enabled and does not crash — so xHCI interrupts were never the hazard the old comment claimed; the timer was, and `sched_active=0` neutralizes it.**

**Why the discarded 1.41.13 `sti` regressed QEMU but this one doesn't.** 1.41.13's `sti` left the scheduler live, so the LAPIC timer preempted the syscall mid-read (do_context_switch on the syscall stack of a non-reentrant kernel) → corruption → `agnsh-type-test` FAILED. 1.41.14 clears `sched_active` first, so the timer can't switch → `agnsh-type-test` **PASS** (exit 0; `help`/`version`/`mode` all register), `agnsh-smoke` PASS, `sweep.sh` **7/7**. Build **1,070,736 B**, multiboot2 OK.

**Hypothesis (this burn).** At `[ASSIST] >`, typing now echoes (agnsh owns echo) and `help`/commands run, because IRQ1 → `kb_isr` delivers exactly as it does for the in-kernel recovery shell on this same box.

**Falsification criteria.** Still no keystrokes → IRQ1 is somehow not reaching `kb_isr` during the syscall (e.g. the legacy PIC was reconfigured/masked off when the LAPIC came up, or IRQ1 is IOAPIC-routed and the in-kernel shell only worked via a path the syscall doesn't share). Next step then: read the boot CMOS/FB to confirm `kb_isr` fires (the in-kernel recovery shell DOES type on this box, so IRQ1 delivery is established — the gap would be syscall-context-specific). A stray char after a line → a pending-IRQ1 edge; benign.

**Read-out (FB):** type `help` at `[ASSIST] >`. PASS = `help` text prints + command echoes. Falsifies = prompt sits dead exactly as before.

#### agnsh typing on iron — `14114` READ the keyboard but every line collapsed to `Command: D` (no echo) → fixed at 1.41.15 (2026-06-05)

**Burn `14114_no_echo_but_input_enter_works.jpg` result.** Big step past `14112`: 1.41.14's IRQ1+`sti` fix **delivered keystrokes** — agnsh reached `[ASSIST] >`, Enter advanced the prompt, and typing produced output. **But two bugs:** (1) **no echo** — typed chars invisible; (2) every typed line dispatched as `Intent: 15 (SHELL_COMMAND)  Command: D  Risk: [MED]`, i.e. the input buffer **collapsed to the single char `D` regardless of what was typed** (one line showed `Intent: 43 (UNKNOWN)`). `'D'`=ASCII 68 = PS/2 scancode `0x20` (the `d` key) with **`kb_shift=1`** — a stuck shift.

**Root cause (verified, adversarial workflow + prior-art).** 1.41.14 routed `read(fd 0)` through IRQ1 correctly, but the read **returned after the FIRST char**, and ring 3 runs **IF=0 between syscalls** (`arch/x86_64/ring3.cyr:108` enters ring 3 with RFLAGS=`0x002`; SYSRET restores it via R11). Combined with agnsh's one-byte-per-`read(0)` loop, each keystroke's scancode stream split across syscalls, and in the **IF=0 inter-byte gap IRQ1 is masked** — so the SHIFT-RELEASE break (`0xAA`) landing there was dropped. `kb_shift` (a global, zeroed only at boot) latched at 1, every letter resolved through `sc_shifted`, and `0x20`→`'D'`; most makes were swallowed in the same gap → single-char collapse. **The in-kernel recovery shell never hits this because it holds IF=1 across the WHOLE line in one loop** (`user/shell.cyr:784-808`) — the decisive control fact. The 5 candidate causes (incl. stuck-HID, firmware-8042-stuck-byte, return-after-first-char ordering) were adversarially refuted against that control; only the IF-gap/lost-break mechanism survives.

**Fix (1.41.15, behavioral, the right layer = stop diverging from the proven reference).** `kbd_read_blocking` becomes a **continuous-IF, line-disciplined reader**: one `sti` window for the WHOLE line (preemption still suspended via `sched_active=0`), drain every make/break in-window so `kb_shift` is always cleared on release, terminate on Enter (`0x20`→`0x0A`), reset `kb_shift`/`kb_ctrl` on entry as defense-in-depth. Echo moves **kernel-side** (the only place it can live once the read is line-buffered — the shell can't see chars until the line completes): printable bytes via `kputc` (serial + GOP fb mirror, the same path `write(1)` uses since 1.41.12), backspace `BS SP BS`, newline on Enter — i.e. the in-kernel shell's echo loop, lifted into the syscall. ABI O1 revised RAW→canonical-lite. `agnoshi` 1.4.1: `read_line` returns one line per call via a carry buffer (target-agnostic — agnos returns one line; host pipes get split). **QEMU-validated:** `agnsh-type-test.py` PASS — `help`/`version`/`mode` now **echo as typed AND dispatch correctly** (no `Command: D`); `agnsh-smoke` PASS (`agnoshi 1.4.1`); `sweep.sh` **7/7**; agnoshi host smoke **59/0**. Build **1,070,960 B**.

**Hypothesis (this burn).** At `[ASSIST] >`, typing now **echoes** each char and full commands dispatch (`help` prints its text, `version` → `agnoshi 1.4.1`, `mode` → `Current mode: AI-ASSIST`), because `read(0)` now drains the whole line under one continuous-IF window exactly like the in-kernel shell that already types correctly on this box.

**Falsification criteria.** Typing still shows a fixed single char per line (e.g. `D`) even with echo on → the keystrokes are NOT reaching `read(0)` correctly despite the continuous-IF window, implicating a deeper producer-layer issue (a genuinely stuck/phantom scancode independent of IF) — but the in-kernel shell's clean per-key delivery makes this unlikely. Chars echo but wrong case / wrong char → a scancode-table or modifier issue, not the IF gap. Nothing echoes at all → a regression in the line-read loop (e.g. it never returns / `kputc` not rendering under exec CR3).

**Read-out (FB):** type `version` then `help` at `[ASSIST] >`. PASS = each char appears as typed, `version` prints `agnoshi 1.4.1`, `help` prints the builtin list — no `Command: D`. Falsifies = a fixed char per line, or dead prompt.

#### agnsh typing on iron — `14115` PASS: line-disciplined `read(0)` types + echoes + dispatches correctly (2026-06-06) ✅ ARC HARDWARE-CLOSE

**Burn `14115_141_miss_labeled.jpg` result — the 1.41.x shell-separation arc's hardware close.** 1.41.15's continuous-IF line-disciplined reader **works on archaemenid**. Boot rode clean to a real DHCP lease (`ACK ip=192.168.1.163 gw=192.168.1.1`, `arp REPLY gw d4:6a:91:ce:70:60`, `net: L2 OK`) → `Launching kybernet` → `kybernet: exec /bin/agnsh` → the agnsh banner + `[ASSIST] >` prompt **FB-visible**. Typing now **echoes as typed AND dispatches correctly** — the hypothesis above is confirmed and the `14114` `Command: D` stuck-shift collapse is gone:
- `help` → printed the full builtins table (help / version / mode / mode auto|assist|human|strict / history / clear / exit,quit).
- `mode` → `Current mode: AI-ASSIST  /  Available: auto, assist, human, strict`.
- `version` → printed the banner version line.

**A1 ✓ (FB-visible banner + prompt), typing ✓ (echo + correct dispatch, no single-char collapse), A4 ✓ (clean exec/storage/net to a real lease).** This validates the whole 1.41.x ladder on iron: shell-separation (agnsh ring-3 from disk) + 1.41.12 stdout FB-mirror + 1.41.12 PMM top-down + 1.41.14 IRQ1/`sti` + 1.41.15 line discipline. **The arc is now iron-complete for its core claim (a userland AI shell, typeable on hardware).**

**Two residuals surfaced — carried to 1.42.x (NOT blockers, the arc's core claim is met):**
1. **Stale banner version (`agnoshi 1.4.0` shown vs 1.4.1 source).** NOT a code bug — `src/agnsh.cyr:40` already reads `agnoshi 1.4.1`. The flashed disk carried a **stale staged binary**: `build/rootfs/bin/agnsh` (Jun 5 04:11) was an `agnoshi 1.4.0` build assembled *before* the 1.41.15 cut bumped agnoshi to 1.4.1 (20:43); the agnos-target build `agnoshi/build/agnsh_agnos` and the smoke seed both already carried 1.4.1. Refreshed the staged binary to 1.4.1 (2026-06-06) so the next flash/seed is correct. **Design follow-up (1.42.x): `VERSION_STR` is a hand-edited literal — derive it from `VERSION` at build time so a bump can't desync the banner again.** Per [[feedback_prefer_mount_modify_over_reflash]] the user can mount agnos-fs and drop the 1.4.1 `/bin/agnsh` in place rather than reflash.
2. **Backspace dead on iron.** The kernel `read(0)` line discipline DOES handle erase (`syscall.cyr:195` `ch==8` → `kputc(8);kputc(32);kputc(8)`) and the scancode table maps backspace `0x0E`→`8` (`keyboard.cyr:124`) — so it works in QEMU but not on this box. Likely the UEFI USB-legacy i8042 emulation on archaemenid delivers a different/extended code for the Backspace key than the make `0x0E` the in-kernel path sees; an iron-only investigation for 1.42.x (the in-kernel recovery shell shares the same erase path — compare its behavior on this box as the control).
